  - **What changed in code.** OpenAI 429 `code=insufficient_quota` now raises `AIProviderQuotaError` (`AI_PROVIDER_QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`). It subclasses `AIServiceUnavailableError`, so every endpoint maps it to 503 (chapter question generation's `handle_question_generation_error` now treats any `AIServiceUnavailableError` as 503, not only retryable errors), and it is not retryable. It is logged at ERROR, which Sentry's default logging integration reports. A real rate limit still retries and returns 429. The staging journey helper now logs status plus `error_code`/`message` (or verdict fields) for the three wizard AI calls. It stops at the first terminal wizard step and names it, so a failure takes about 15s instead of 1.6 minutes and the message lists the responses.
  - **Verified.** `test_provider_quota.py` stubs only the OpenAI wire. Locally against a wire stub: `insufficient_quota` gave `generate-questions` 503 `AI_PROVIDER_QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` after one upstream call, and `rate_limit_exceeded` gave 429 `AI_RATE_LIMIT` after three. Staging still runs the 2026-08-28 build, so it will keep showing `AI_RATE_LIMIT` until it is redeployed.

- **Only the user's current Stripe subscription moves the plan, and checkout refuses a second one (#768, P0.20)**:
  - **The bug.** The webhook never compared an event's subscription id with the stored `stripe_subscription_id`. A user paying on subscription A who cancelled a duplicate B was downgraded to free. Checkout blocked only `plan == "pro"`, so two tabs, or a past_due subscriber on free, could open a second subscription.
  - **The rule.** The webhook now stores `stripe_subscription_status` beside the id. "Live" means `active`, `trialing` or `past_due`: `entitlements.LIVE_SUBSCRIPTION_STATUSES` derives it from `SUBSCRIPTION_STATUS_PLAN`, and `has_live_subscription(user)` reads it. A pre-#768 doc with a subscription id but no status counts as live when it is on pro, since only an active or trialing subscription ever granted pro. Without this, a duplicate's cancellation would still downgrade existing subscribers on deploy day (raised by the opencode/GLM review).
    - An event for another subscription is a logged no-op if it is a deletion, or if the current subscription is still live. The response is `status: not_current_subscription`.
    - If the current subscription is not live (none yet, `unpaid`, `incomplete_expired`, `canceled`, or a pre-#768 doc not on pro), the other subscription becomes current. This is the re-upgrade path.
    - A second live subscription is logged at ERROR, because the user is being double-billed and needs a refund.
    - An ignored event releases its replay marker, as `no_matching_user` already did, so a dashboard Resend still applies once its subscription becomes current.
  - **#902 GLM finding folded in.** A `past_due` (keep) write sets no plan, and it no longer advances the `stripe_event_created` watermark. Before, a resent older `active` event whose first delivery was lost was rejected as `stale_event`, and the paying user stayed on free. The ordering filter still guards the keep write itself, so a late past_due cannot overwrite newer state.
  - **Account deletion (#764).** The cancel step clears the subscription id, so the webhook now ignores Stripe's `subscription.deleted` for that subscription. The cancel therefore applies the deletion's effect itself: plan free, status cleared, and `stripe_event_created` stamped with the subscription's `canceled_at`. This matters when a later deletion step fails and the account survives. Without the watermark, a retried pre-cancel `updated(active)` for the dead subscription would re-establish it and grant pro permanently. That case was raised as a Major by the `glm-review` bot.
  - **Checkout.** Returns 409 "You already have a subscription. Use Manage billing to update or cancel it." while a live subscription exists. `unpaid`, `incomplete`, `incomplete_expired` and `canceled` still pass, since that is the re-upgrade path.
  - **Verified.**
    - 19 signed-webhook tests on real Mongo in the new `test_stripe_webhook_current_subscription.py`, plus 8 checkout cases. Branch diff coverage is 100%.
    - Ten mutations, each caught by at least one test: a deletion establishing, a live current subscription not blocking, the keep write advancing the watermark, the marker kept on ignore, the missing duplicate ERROR, status not stored, past_due not live, the checkout guard removed, a deletion keeping its status, and dropping the pre-#768 pro fallback.
    - Real-HTTP demo against the backend, using a local Stripe wire stub. A stale `sub_B` deletion left a sub_A subscriber on pro. Checkout returned 409 while sub_A was active, and the stub logged zero calls. past_due followed by a resent older active gave pro.
  - **Known limits.**
    - The watermark is still per user. A new subscription's event that is older than the previous subscription's deletion is rejected until that subscription's next event. Bypassing the watermark instead would let a late event from a dead subscription grant pro permanently.
    - Two checkout tabs opened before any webhook lands can still produce two subscriptions. The second is ignored and logged at ERROR.
- **`ENVIRONMENT` fails closed: no compose default, unknown values refuse to start (#777, P0.29)**: `docker-compose.yml` defaulted `ENVIRONMENT` to `staging`, and `is_production_env()` matches only `production`, so a forgotten marker or `prod` quietly switched off the BYPASS_AUTH hard-block and the weak-secret checks. Compose now uses `${ENVIRONMENT:?...}`, and `Settings` has an `ENVIRONMENT` field that refuses to build unless the value is one of development/test/staging/production (lower-cased to match the guards' #309 normalization; missing, empty, `prod`, `dev` and padded values are rejected). It defaults to `None` and is validated rather than declared required, because pydantic's "missing" error prints every other setting (Mongo URI, API keys) as `input_value` into the startup log. The field reads `backend/.env` but the guards read only `os.environ`, so a value set only in `.env` is lifted into `os.environ` at import. Without that, `ENVIRONMENT=production` in `.env` validates with the guards off; on main that same `.env` started with BYPASS_AUTH active and served `/api/v1/books` unauthenticated. The staging deploy and `sync-staging-env` workflows now `export ENVIRONMENT=staging` (the PM2 workflow also hardcoded it), as does the deploy's failure-path `compose logs`, which interpolates the files too and already lacked `IMAGE_TAG`. The CI E2E backend sets `ENVIRONMENT: test`, and `conftest.py` forces `test`. `tests/test_core/test_environment_777.py` covers each value in `Settings()` and in a fresh interpreter importing `app.core.config`. `scripts/test_compose_environment_required.py` pins the compose value and checks that every workflow running `compose up` exports the marker. **Local dev:** add `ENVIRONMENT=development` to `backend/.env` (now in `.env.example`) or the backend will not start.
- **Support/privacy/2FA-recovery address moved off the parked autoauthor.com domain (#773, P0.25)**: every published address was `support@autoauthor.com`, a parked domain with no MX record, so privacy/data requests and 2FA-recovery mail bounced. Now one constant (`frontend/src/lib/constants/contact.ts`, `SUPPORT_EMAIL`) = `support@autoauthor.app`, used by `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/dashboard/help`, `verify-2fa` and their tests; the user manual and troubleshooting doc updated. `SupportAddressGate.test.ts` fails if `autoauthor.com` reappears in `frontend/src` or the address is hardcoded outside the constant. Historic changelog/demo/archive text left as written. **Owner homework (issue stays open):** provision the `support@autoauthor.app` mailbox + MX record, then send a test message.
- **Production session cookies can no longer reach the shared staging box (#778, P0.30, security)**:
  - **The bug.** `getCookieDomain()` returned `.${host}` for whatever host `BETTER_AUTH_URL` named. A production frontend on the apex would issue its session cookie for `.autoauthor.app`, which RFC 6265 domain-matches `dev.autoauthor.app` and `api.dev.autoauthor.app`, both on the shared staging box.
  - **The fix.** An apex (two-label host) is never widened. It gets a host-only cookie with no `Domain` attribute, which the browser sends to no subdomain, and the server logs a warning that an `api.<apex>` backend will not receive it. Subdomain hosts are unchanged: staging keeps `Domain=.dev.autoauthor.app`. Cookie names are untouched, so the backend reader needed no change and nothing overlaps #777 (this reads only the auth URL, never `ENVIRONMENT`).
  - **What production must do.** Run the frontend on a subdomain (`app.autoauthor.app` with the API at `api.app.autoauthor.app`, mirroring staging), or serve the API from the apex's own host. Written into `.github/DEPLOYMENT.md`, `docs/session-management.md` and `docs/production-readiness-gaps.md`, which had prescribed the apex plus `api.autoauthor.app` layout.
  - **Tests.** RFC 6265 domain-match pins show neither production layout (apex, subdomain) reaches either staging host, a subdomain frontend still reaches its own `api.` host, and the staging cookie reaches both staging hosts but not the apex. The old pin that asserted `.autoauthor.app` was the bug and now uses a subdomain. Removing the apex guard fails 2 pins. Making every host host-only fails 5.
  - **Verified.** `next build` and `next start` with production env, real sign-up and sign-in against local Mongo. Apex on main gives `Domain=.autoauthor.app`. Apex on the fix gives no `Domain` attribute. `app.autoauthor.app` gives `Domain=.app.autoauthor.app` and `dev.autoauthor.app` gives `Domain=.dev.autoauthor.app`. The staging-config cookie, presented under its issued name to the real backend, returns 200 on `/users/me` (401 without it).
  - **Limitation.** Apex detection counts labels, so a multi-label public suffix (`example.co.uk`) would read as a subdomain. That doesn't apply to `.app`. Switch to a public-suffix list before hosting on such a TLD.

- **The frontend container no longer receives the backend's secrets (#781, P0.33, security)**:
  - **The bug.** The frontend service used the backend's `env_file: .env`, so the Next.js process held every OpenAI, Stripe, AWS and Cloudinary key plus the backend Sentry DSN, none of which it reads. An SSR RCE like the one patched in #738 would have exposed billing and cloud credentials.
  - **The fix.** `docker-compose.yml` drops the frontend's `env_file` for an explicit `environment:` list: the Mongo connection, `BETTER_AUTH_*`, the `EMAIL_*` password-reset settings, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT` and the Mongo retry knobs. Values are still interpolated from the one box `.env`, so no second file on the box. Optional names are bare `KEY:` entries, which pass a value through when set and leave it unset when not, so code fallbacks still apply. `docker compose config` with a staging-shaped `.env` went from 27 frontend variables to 13.
  - **Guards.** `scripts/test_frontend_env_allowlist.py` fails if any compose file gives the frontend an `env_file`, a name `frontend/src` never reads (plus `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`/`NODE_ENV`, read implicitly), `BYPASS_AUTH`/`E2E_ALLOW_BYPASS`, or a backend-only name. All five mutations tried were caught. The staging deploy gained a **Frontend carries no backend secret** step that runs `env` in the running container and fails on any backend-only name (names only, never values, in the log).
  - **Not covered.** A new server-side variable the frontend starts reading has to be added to the list by hand, or it is absent in the container. AC2 on staging itself is confirmed by the next staging deploy's new step.
- **A `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` published in a doc no longer boots a deployed server (#780, P0.32)**:
  - **The bug.** `docs/better-auth-migration-guide.md` gave a concrete 43-character `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` as the value to use. That secret signs better-auth's session-cookie cache, so any environment that copied it accepts forged cache cookies. Neither the backend weak-secret list nor the frontend knew about it.
  - **The fix.** The doc uses a placeholder. `backend/app/core/config.py` and `frontend/src/lib/auth-secret.ts` carry a deny-list of SHA-256 hashes, never the literal, and refuse a match when `ENVIRONMENT`/`NODE_ENV` is production, prod or staging (backend validator, frontend `register()` in `instrumentation.ts`). Local development and CI may still use it. `Settings` now sets `hide_input_in_errors`, because pydantic echoed the rejected secret into the startup traceback. A guard test hashes every token in every tracked file, so the value cannot come back.
  - **Not here.** Rotating every environment that used the value (including local `backend/.env` and the staging box) is the owner's job. The value is still in git history; rewriting history is an owner decision.
- **Deleting an account cancels its Stripe subscription (#764, P0.16)**:
  - **The bug.** Both delete routes cascaded the books and soft-deleted the user, but nothing in `backend/app` called `stripe.Subscription.cancel`. A Pro user who deleted their account kept being charged and could no longer reach the billing portal to stop it.
  - **The fix.** `billing.cancel_subscription_for_deletion(auth_id)` runs first in `DELETE /users/me` and `DELETE /users/{auth_id}`, before the book cascade and the soft delete. It reads the user doc (so the admin route cancels the target's subscription, not the caller's) and, when `stripe_subscription_id` is set, cancels it via `asyncio.to_thread` with idempotency key `account-delete-<sub id>`. A `StripeError` returns 502 and nothing has been deleted, so the user can retry. After a successful cancel the id is cleared, so a retry after a later step fails does not cancel a cancelled subscription again (the idempotency key only lasts 24h). The duplicate admin route got the same fix; removing it stays with #851.
  - **UI.** The delete dialog says any paid subscription is cancelled immediately, and the 502's detail is what the "Delete failed" toast shows.
  - **Tests.** `test_account_deletion_stripe.py` stubs Stripe at the HTTP boundary: a local server stands in for api.stripe.com through `stripe.api_base`, so the real SDK sends the request. Real Mongo throughout. Covered on both routes: one cancel with the stored id and key, a failure leaves the account active with its books and id, no subscription means no request, and the retry case.
  - **Not covered.** A subscription already cancelled in Stripe whose `customer.subscription.deleted` webhook has not landed yet still has its id stored. If Stripe rejects cancelling it again, deletion returns 502 until the webhook clears the id.
- **Checkout now discloses auto-renewal and records the user's consent (#770, P0.22, CA ARL / ROSCA)**:
  - **The gap.** The Terms said fees and cancellation terms are "presented at the point of purchase". The Upgrade button showed no price, no billing period, no auto-renewal statement and no way to cancel, and `Session.create` set neither `consent_collection` nor any renewal `custom_text`.
  - **One source for the terms.** `RENEWAL_DISCLOSURE_TEMPLATE` and `RENEWAL_DISCLOSURE_VERSION` in `billing.py` are the only copy. New `GET /billing/disclosure` renders the template from the live Stripe Price (`stripe.Price.retrieve(STRIPE_PRICE_ID_PRO)`, cached per price id because Stripe prices are immutable), so the advertised price can't drift from the charge. It returns `{version, text, sha256, price_id}`. Unconfigured is 503, a Stripe failure is 502, and a non-recurring price is 503.
  - **Consent before checkout.** `BillingSettingsForm` shows the text in a bordered box with the Upgrade button and an unchecked "I agree to these automatic renewal terms and the Terms of Service" checkbox. Upgrade stays disabled until the box is ticked, and while the terms can't load (a Try again button refetches them). `POST /billing/checkout` now requires `accept_renewal_terms: true` plus the `disclosure_sha256` the user saw. Missing or declined consent is 400. A hash that no longer matches the current text (the price or copy changed) is 409, and the form reloads the new terms and clears the tick. Neither case reaches Stripe.
  - **The record.** Before any Stripe call, `audit_request` writes `billing.renewal_consent` to `audit_logs` with the actor, timestamp, IP, user-agent, request id, disclosure version, sha256, full text and price id.
  - **Stripe's page.** `Session.create` now passes `consent_collection={"terms_of_service": "required"}`, the renewal text as `custom_text.submit`, and a `terms_of_service_acceptance` message linking `/terms`.
  - **Terms section 6** now says subscriptions renew automatically until cancelled, that you cancel online under Settings → Billing → Manage billing, and that cancellation takes effect at the end of the current period. Last-updated date bumped.
  - **Owner homework.** Stripe rejects `consent_collection.terms_of_service` unless a Terms of Service URL is set in Dashboard → Settings → Public details, so set it before deploying (now noted in `backend/.env.example`). The wording is plain-language placeholder copy; counsel sign-off is #791. The "end of the current period" promise depends on #771 pinning the portal to at-period-end cancellation.
- **Removing a chapter deletes its questions, responses and ratings (#755, P0.7)**:
  - **The bug.** Questions live in their own collection keyed by `(book_id, chapter_id, user_id)`, and responses and ratings hang off the question id. Neither `PUT /toc` nor `DELETE /books/{id}/chapters/{cid}` touched them, so a removed chapter's Q&A stayed behind for any later chapter with the same id and fed its draft generation. The delete endpoint's docstring claimed it already cascaded.
  - **The fix.** Both writers diff the stored chapter ids (the whole tree, so subchapters count) against what they wrote and call the new `delete_questions_for_chapters`, which deletes the removed chapters' questions scoped by book and user, plus their responses and ratings. Accepting a regenerated TOC goes through `PUT /toc`, so it is covered. The cascade runs only after the version-guarded write commits, so a 409 or 404 deletes nothing. Like the audit row it is best-effort and logged at error level: the edit has already committed, and anything a failed cascade leaves behind cannot re-attach, because since #754 the server re-mints every chapter id it has not stored. The docstring now says what happens.
  - **By design.** A `PUT /toc` that keeps an item carrying stored id X is keeping X, with its draft (#749) and its Q&A. Sending X's id is how a client says "this is X".
  - **Verified.** `test_toc_removal_cascade.py` runs on real Mongo; 5 of its 7 tests fail on main. It covers a PUT drop with a subchapter, scoping to another book and another user, remove-then-re-add of X showing zero questions, a 409 deleting nothing, DELETE with a subchapter, a 404 deleting nothing, and a generate-toc accept. Six mutations are caught: no cascade on PUT, no cascade on DELETE, responses kept, ratings kept, user scope dropped, subchapters missed. Over real HTTP, with questions from the real generate-questions endpoint fed by a local OpenAI stub, every path leaves 0/0/0 for the removed chapters while kept chapters stay at 3/1/1.
- **The Stripe webhook reads the subscription's status, so lapsed subscriptions stop granting Pro (#767, P0.19)**:
  - **The bug.** `_apply_subscription_event` mapped the price to a plan and never read `subscription["status"]`. An `incomplete`, `past_due`, `unpaid`, `paused` or `incomplete_expired` subscription with the Pro price kept granting Pro, and nothing ever wrote `restricted`, so the billing page's "subscription inactive" branch could not be reached. An unknown price, or an unset `STRIPE_PRICE_ID_PRO`, quietly moved a paying user to free with an INFO log.
  - **The policy.** One table, `entitlements.SUBSCRIPTION_STATUS_PLAN`, read by the pure `plan_for_subscription(status, price_ids)`. `active` and `trialing` get the plan the price buys. `past_due` leaves the plan as it is: a Pro user keeps Pro while Stripe retries the renewal, and a free user is not upgraded. `unpaid` sets `restricted`. `incomplete`, `incomplete_expired`, `paused` and `canceled` set `free`, and so does any status Stripe adds later. `incomplete_expired` deviates from the issue's proposed `restricted` for the owner to confirm: the first payment never went through, so the user was never Pro, and restricting them would leave a declined checkout worse off than never trying. The `subscription.deleted` event still sets free.
  - **Logging.** An active or trialing subscription with no recognised price logs at ERROR with the price ids, and so does an unknown status.
  - **Tests.** Signed webhooks on real Mongo, one per status, for a Pro subscriber. Two more cases cover past_due on a free or restricted user, plus a dunning cycle (active, past_due, unpaid, active). Three ERROR-log tests: an unknown price, an unset pro price and an unknown status. A fourth confirms a matched price logs no ERROR. Seven mutations, each failing at least one test: past_due, unpaid, incomplete, the unknown-status default, both ERROR logs, and writing `plan=None`.
  - **Not here.** Events for a subscription that is not the user's current one, and a second subscription created at checkout, are #768. Claiming the idempotency marker before applying is #769.
- **The chapter editor sends its save token and asks reload-or-overwrite on a conflict (#760, P0.12)**:
  - **The bug.** Every content save was unconditional. Two tabs or devices on one chapter silently overwrote each other, last write wins. #759 gave the PATCH an optional `expected_last_modified` precondition, and no client sent it.
  - **The fix.**
    - `saveChapterContent` takes `expectedLastModified`. When it is `undefined` the key is left out and the save is unconditional. `null`, for a chapter never saved, is sent. A 409 with the backend's detail throws an error carrying `currentLastModified` and `currentContent`.
    - Debounced autosave, manual Save and the keepalive flush on leaving all go through one helper, `saveChapterEdit`. It sends the token last seen for that chapter verbatim, then records the token from the 200 before resolving. That means a flush waiting on an in-flight save sends the newer token. Tokens come from GET content only, never `/chapters/metadata`.
    - On a 409 the editor keeps the writer's text and backs it up to localStorage. It stops autosaving, disables Save, and asks in the existing `role="alert"` region. **Overwrite with mine** resends the current text on `current_last_modified`, only on click. **Reload their version** backs the local text up again, edits made since the conflict included, then loads the server copy. The Restore Backup banner then offers the dropped text. The dropped text has its own storage key (`chapter-dropped-…`), so a later save neither clears it nor, if the save fails, overwrites it. It stays until the writer restores or dismisses it. If the browser refuses to store it, for example at quota, Reload does not replace anything: the text stays in the editor and the alert asks the writer to copy it.
    - A flush on leaving during an unresolved conflict still sends the old token. It gets a 409 and lands in the backup, so leaving never overwrites the other copy.
    - A conflict is cleared when the editor loads another chapter. A save that 409s after a swap to another chapter is backed up under its own chapter and raises nothing on the new one. Together these mean a caller that swaps `chapterId` without remounting is not left with Save disabled. A 409 without `current_last_modified` is treated as a plain failure, so the resend below can never become an unconditional save.
    - The bulk-status endpoint moves `last_modified` without touching content. A 409 whose `current_content` equals the text this editor last saw is therefore resent once on the new token, with no prompt. Without that, every status change from the tab menu would raise a false conflict.
  - **Verified.**
    - 17 real-TipTap jest tests in `ChapterEditor.conflict.test.tsx` and 4 bookClient tests were added. All of the editor tests fail on main.
    - Eleven mutations were checked and all eleven were caught: dropping the token, autosaving during a conflict, removing the status-only resend, resending on every conflict, skipping the backup on Reload, clearing the Reload backup on a later save, keeping a conflict across a chapter change, accepting a 409 with no token, replacing the text when its backup failed, raising a late 409 on the next chapter, and sharing one key between the dropped text and failed-save backups.
    - The opencode (GLM) review raised two Majors, the cleared Reload backup and the conflict surviving a chapter swap, plus one Suggestion, the 409 with no token. All three are fixed above. The `glm-review` bot's second round raised one Major, the unchecked Reload backup, and two minors, the late 409 after a swap and a failed save overwriting the dropped text. All three are fixed too.
    - Browser demo with two contexts against a real backend and Mongo. A saved, then B typed. B got a 409, showed the choice, and kept its text and a backup, while Mongo still held A's text after 5 more seconds of typing in B. Overwrite put B's text in Mongo. A, now stale, got a conflict, and Reload showed B's text with A's text in the backup, leaving Mongo unchanged. A status change followed by typing saved with no prompt.
  - **Left for later.** `ChapterEditor.tsx` was already over the 500-line limit before this change and is now at 817 lines.
- **TOC generation no longer truncates typical TOCs into an always-failing retryable error (#774, P0.26)**:
  - **The bug.** `generate_toc_from_summary_and_responses` asked for `max_tokens=1500`, but the prompt requests 6-12 chapters x 2-4 subchapters as pretty JSON (about 2k tokens at 8x3, 3.7k at 12x4). The cut-off JSON could not parse and the user got `AI_INVALID_RESPONSE retryable=true`; every retry failed identically and spent quota.
  - **The fix.** Budget is now `TOC_MAX_COMPLETION_TOKENS = 6000`. A `finish_reason == "length"` response raises `AI_RESPONSE_TRUNCATED` with `retryable=false` (one API call, no retries).
  - **Verified.** Through the real `generate-toc` endpoint against an OpenAI-compatible wire stub that truncates when `max_tokens` is below what the TOC needs: main returned `AI_INVALID_RESPONSE` (stub saw 1500), the branch returned the full 10x3 TOC (stub saw 6000), and an oversized 25x4 TOC returned `AI_RESPONSE_TRUNCATED retryable=false`. Tests in `test_toc_token_budget.py`.

- **Deleting an account now actually signs it out everywhere (#763, P0.15)**:
  - **The bug.** `DELETE /users/me` cascaded the books and set `is_active: False`, but nothing read that flag. The better-auth `user`, `account`, `session` and `twoFactor` documents survived, so the same cookie kept returning 200 on `/users/me` and 201 on `POST /books`, and the old password still signed in. A session whose better-auth user row was gone also still authenticated as long as the app record existed.
  - **The fix.** `get_current_user_from_session` returns 401 when the better-auth user is missing, and when the app record has `is_active is False`. The check is an explicit False, so records that predate the field stay active. `delete_user` is the one function both delete endpoints call. It now purges the user's better-auth `session`, `account`, `twoFactor` and `user` docs, matching `userId` both as an ObjectId (what the adapter writes) and as a string. The profile page calls `authClient.signOut()` after the DELETE succeeds and before redirecting.
  - **Found while fixing it: re-signup with the same email would 500.** The retained soft-deleted record kept its email, so with the better-auth user gone, a new sign-up's auto-create hit `email_unique_idx`. The soft delete now `$unset`s the email. `UserResponse.email` is Optional so `/users/admin/users` still serialises deleted records.
  - **Verified.** A real-session integration suite (no dependency override, real Mongo) fails on main. After deletion both devices' cookies get 401, no better-auth docs remain, a session minted afterwards for the old id gets 401, and the same email signs up fresh. The demo ran against real better-auth: sign-up, a second device, 2FA enable, delete through the UI. Afterwards every better-auth doc for the user was gone, both cookies got 401, and sign-in with the old password returned `INVALID_EMAIL_OR_PASSWORD`.
  - **Left for #764:** Stripe subscription cancellation on delete. It belongs beside `delete_user`.
- **Chapter ids are unique and server-minted, so one autosave edits one chapter (#754, P0.6)**:
  - **The bug.** The chapter autosave writes with `array_filters=[{"c.id": id}]`, which updates every element carrying that id. Edit TOC numbered new chapters `ch${toc.length + 1}` and subchapters `${parentId}-${n + 1}`, so delete-then-add stored `[ch1, ch3, ch3]` and one autosave overwrote both chapters. `PUT /toc` filled missing ids but never checked for repeats. Since #749 the draft carry also matches by id, so a new chapter reusing a removed chapter's id, or an accepted AI proposal whose positional `ch1` matched a stored `ch1`, inherited an unrelated draft.
  - **The fix.** `PUT /toc` rejects an id repeated anywhere in the tree with a 400 before writing, so the stored TOC is unchanged. It then replaces every id the server has not stored with a uuid4, before the #749 carry runs, so a client id only ever names a stored chapter. add-chapter always mints a uuid4 and refuses (400) to extend a tree that already holds duplicates. generate-toc replaces the AI's positional ids with uuid4s in the proposal. `_walk_toc` is now recursive, so the checks and the carry cover any depth a client sends. Edit TOC mints ids with `crypto.randomUUID()`.
  - **Contract change.** Two tests pinned "an unknown client id is kept as sent" (`test_update_toc_creates_toc_and_assigns_ids`, `test_stored_value_wins_and_new_items_keep_their_own`). They now assert the server-minted id.
  - **Not covered.** Books that already store duplicate ids are not repaired. `PUT /toc` re-sending them and add-chapter both get a 400 until one of the duplicates is deleted on the Edit TOC page.
- **AI quotas are now per plan, so Pro buys something (#766, P0.18)**:
  - **The bug.** `free` and `pro` had identical entitlements and `get_ai_usage_quota` used one global 50/day, 500/month cap and never read `plan`, while the billing page sold "full access to every AI writing feature" that Free already had. A Pro user at the cap was told to contact support.
  - **The fix.** New settings `AI_QUOTA_FREE_DAILY/MONTHLY` (10/100) and `AI_QUOTA_PRO_DAILY/MONTHLY` (50/500) replace `AI_QUOTA_DAILY_LIMIT/MONTHLY_LIMIT`. `entitlements.ai_quota_for_plan` is the one lookup: a missing plan is `free`, and `restricted` or any unknown plan gets no allowance (402, the same upgrade panel as the entitlement gate). The 429 detail now states the cap, the reset time and, for non-Pro, what Pro raises it to; "contact support" is gone. Stale "no payment provider yet" / "swap to plan when P0.2 lands" comments removed.
  - **One source for the copy.** New `GET /billing/quotas` returns the per-plan caps from the same settings; `BillingSettingsForm` renders "Free: 10 ... Pro: 50 ..." from it (and the Pro user's own cap). The TOC wizard no longer swallows a quota 429 on analyze: `ErrorDisplay` shows the cap with a link to the billing tab and no retry. A plain per-endpoint rate-limit 429 keeps its retry path.
  - **Not here.** Mapping Stripe subscription statuses to plans is #767.
- **One user can no longer lock another out by claiming their email (#765, P0.17)**:
  - **The bug.** `UserUpdate.email` went straight onto the unique `users.email` index, unverified. An attacker set their address to the victim's. On the victim's first sign-in, auto-create hit `DuplicateKeyError`, assumed an auth_id race, re-fetched nothing and returned 500 on every request, permanently. The 409 "Email already exists" was an account-existence oracle, the diverged email fed `stripe.Customer.create`, and a case variant could match the staging E2E exemption.
  - **The fix.** `email` is gone from `UserUpdate`, so the existing `extra="forbid"` makes it a 422. The dead 409 branch is deleted. better-auth owns the email: `user.changeEmail` stays at its default (disabled), so no flow changes it today. On every authenticated request the session path copies better-auth's email onto the users doc when the two differ, which also repairs docs that were already squatted. On `DuplicateKeyError`, auto-create re-fetches by auth_id. If a record comes back, it is the #178 race and that record is reused. If none comes back, the email index fired. That is logged as a warning, `release_email` unsets the address on the stale doc (better-auth guarantees it belongs to this user), and the insert is retried once. The branch keys on whether a winner exists, not on the error's `keyPattern`, so a differently shaped error cannot fall back into the lockout.
  - **Tests rewritten.** The mocked test that pinned the 500 now covers only the unresolvable case: the retry collides again with no winner. `test_email_ownership.py` covers the lockout itself on real Mongo, with a real better-auth `user`/`session` and a real cookie: first sign-in past a squatter, five concurrent first loads producing one record, the mirror (which also writes an audit entry), and the mirror releasing a squatted address. `email` joins the #244 privileged-field 422 matrix.
  - **Verified.** Over real HTTP: on main the attacker's PATCH returns 200 and the victim gets 500 twice. On the fix, against the same poisoned database, the victim gets 200, the squatter's email is released, the attacker's next request restores their own address, and a repeat PATCH returns 422. Breaking the release, the mirror, the winner check, or putting `email` back in `UserUpdate` each fails at least one test.
  - **Not here.** A user-facing change-email flow (better-auth `changeEmail` plus a verification email and a settings UI) is a separate feature. The mirror picks it up without further backend work.

- **`Permissions-Policy` no longer blocks the shipped voice input (#772, P0.24)**: `next.config.ts` sent `microphone=()` on every route, an empty allowlist that refuses `getUserMedia` and SpeechRecognition for the app's own origin with no browser-side fix. It is now `camera=(), microphone=(self), geolocation=()`; camera and geolocation stay off and third-party frames still get no mic. It was the only place the header is set (middleware, backend and deploy config checked). `src/__tests__/nextConfigHeaders.test.ts` pins the value from the real `headers()` export. CI could not catch this before because Jest mocks media and the E2E specs skip the microphone.
- **A background TOC refresh or a session refetch no longer remounts the chapter editor (#758, P0.10)**:
  - **The bugs.**
    - `useChapterTabs.refreshChapters` set `is_loading`, and `ChapterTabs` shows its skeleton while loading. Every `tocUpdated` event and every cross-tab `toc-updated-<book>` storage event (a TOC saved on the Edit TOC page, a chapter created or deleted) unmounted the editor. The cursor, focus and undo history were lost, and the remount fetched the chapter content again.
    - `refreshChapters` read the active tab from its render closure, so a tab picked while a refresh was in flight was switched back when the refresh landed. A failed refresh set `error`, which replaced the editor with the error panel.
    - The book page keyed its load on the `session` object. better-auth hands out a new object on every session refetch for the same user, including on window focus, so the page went back to its skeleton and unmounted `ChapterTabs`.
    - Both were reproduced in a real browser on main. After a TOC save in a second tab, or a `visibilitychange`, the skeleton showed, the editor's DOM node was replaced, the chapter content was fetched 3 more times, and keystrokes typed afterwards were lost.
  - **The fix.**
    - `refreshChapters` is a background refresh. It never touches `is_loading`, and it merges the new chapter list into `prev` inside the state updater, so its deps are just `[bookId]`.
    - A failed refresh keeps the chapters already loaded. The error panel, with its Retry, appears only when nothing is loaded.
    - The book page keys its load and its settled check on `session?.user?.id`. A different user still reloads the page.
  - **Verified.**
    - `ChapterTabs.backgroundRefresh.test.tsx` runs the real `ChapterTabs`, hook, `useTocSync` and TipTap editor. Four of its five tests fail on main. The fifth pins the error panel when nothing is loaded.
    - `page.sessionIdentity.test.tsx` checks two cases: a new session object for the same user does not refetch the book or remount `ChapterTabs` (fails on main), and a different user does reload.
    - Mutating the active tab back to the render closure fails the mid-refresh test.
    - Browser demo against a real backend, Mongo and better-auth, run before and after the fix. With the fix the same editor node survived both triggers, with no skeleton, no content GET and no book GET. Keystrokes typed afterwards landed, and Mongo stored them.
  - **Left for later.** A single mount still requests the chapter content more than once. The first request starts before TipTap's instance exists, and its result is discarded. That predates this issue. Save conflicts are #760.
- **The chapter editor no longer drops edits or saves over a chapter it failed to load (#757, P0.9)**:
  - **The bugs.**
    - Effect cleanup only cleared the autosave timer. Since #756 keys the editor per chapter, text typed inside the 3s debounce before a tab switch was dropped on unmount.
    - A successful save cleared `autoSavePending`/`hasUnsavedChanges` unconditionally, so text typed while the PATCH was in flight was never saved and raised no `beforeunload` warning.
    - A failed content load left an empty, editable editor, and the first autosave replaced the real chapter. A legacy "tab state" branch did the same thing deliberately.
    - Each was reproduced in a real browser against real Mongo on main. In the switch case nothing was saved. In the held-save case Mongo kept only the first burst. When the GET returned 500, typing autosaved `<p>Overwrite attempt.</p>` over the chapter.
  - **The fix.**
    - `onUpdate` records the latest unsaved edit as `{bookId, chapterId, content}` at edit time. It is cleared when the HTML matches the saved copy or a load replaces it.
    - A cleanup keyed on `[bookId, chapterId]` flushes that edit to its own chapter. It uses the new `keepalive` option on `saveChapterContent`, which falls back to a normal request above the browser's 64KB keepalive limit. If the flush fails, the edit becomes the chapter's localStorage backup.
    - The flush waits for any save still in flight. Otherwise the older PATCH could land last, and its success handler could delete the flush's backup. Internal review caught this.
    - Autosave and manual save clear the pending flags only when `editor.getHTML()` still equals what was sent. Otherwise the effect re-arms for the trailing edit.
    - A failed load sets `loadFailed`. The editor becomes read-only, the alert gains a Retry button, and the toolbar, Restore Backup and Save are withheld. Autosave, manual save and the flush all refuse to write.
    - The three copy-pasted backup blocks are now one helper in `chapterContentSave.ts`.
  - **Verified.**
    - Nine new real-TipTap jest tests and one bookClient keepalive test were added. Seven of the editor tests fail against main's `ChapterEditor.tsx`. The other two pin what the flush must not do: send a reverted edit, or save a chapter nobody edited.
    - 13 mutations were checked and 11 were caught. The two survivors are a flush that reads the cleanup's own `chapterId`, which is equivalent because that closure belongs to the chapter being left, and the autosave `loadFailed` gate. That gate only matters for a draft inserted from the Questions tab, which the tests do not drive.
    - The browser demo, run before and after, stored the right text in Mongo for all three scenarios, plus a fourth: typing during a held save and then switching tabs.
  - **Left for later.** A `pagehide` flush is out of scope: React does not unmount on unload, and the `beforeunload` warning is accurate again. Remounts on a background TOC refresh are #758, and save conflicts are #760.
- **Parallel question saves no longer 500, and `edit_history` stops growing (#762, P0.14)**:
  - **The bug.** `save_question_response`, `save_question_responses_batch` and `save_question_rating` each read the existing document, then inserted or updated it. Concurrent first saves all read nothing, all inserted, and every loser hit the unique `question_user_idx` as a DuplicateKeyError: a 500 from `PUT .../response` and `POST .../rating`, a failed item from the batch endpoint. Each response save also appended to `metadata.edit_history` with no cap. Reproduced over real HTTP on main with 50 parallel first saves per path: 17 of 50 response PUTs and 6 of 50 rating POSTs returned 500, 3 of 50 batch items failed, and 100 later saves left 102 history entries.
  - **The fix.** All three paths go through one helper, `_upsert_one`: a single `find_one_and_update` with `$set`, `$setOnInsert` (`_id`, `created_at`) and `upsert=True`, retried once on DuplicateKeyError. Response saves add `$push {$each, $slice: -50}` (`EDIT_HISTORY_LIMIT`). Re-rating no longer overwrites a rating's `created_at`. MongoDB 4.2+ retries a duplicate-key upsert server-side when the filter is pure equality on the unique index, as ours is, so the client retry is the fallback for servers or filters where it does not. A test with a non-equality filter forces the client path; measured locally, without the retry that burst surfaces about 1.4 duplicate-key errors per 50 upserts.
  - **Contract change: one `edit_history` entry per save.** An entry is `{timestamp, word_count}` for the text being saved, and the first save now gets one too. The old entry held the *previous* save's word count, which a single atomic `$push` cannot read. Nothing renders `edit_history`; the frontend only types it. `test_batch_save_edit_history_tracking` now asserts the new contract.
  - **Verified.** Seven new real-Mongo tests, including 20 parallel `PUT .../response` through the ASGI app (all 200, one document), 20 parallel ratings and batches (one document each), and 100 saves (50 entries, the newest). All six behavior tests fail on main; the seventh pins the retry, and removing the retry fails it. Demo over real HTTP against a local backend, same script on main and on the branch: the branch returned 200 for all 50 of each, stored one document per path, and kept history at 50 entries, word counts 51..100.

- **Question autosave no longer loops while idle or downgrades a completed answer (#761, P0.13)**:
  - **The bug.** The autosave effect in `QuestionDisplay` re-armed on every `saveStatus` change and never compared the text to what the server held. Every save is `DRAFT`, so merely opening a completed answer re-saved it as a draft every ~3s, each time costing a PUT, a verification GET and a parent callback. A jest probe (one act per second, as a browser re-runs effects between ticks) counted 13 saves in 60s of idle on main.
  - **The fix.** A `lastSavedTextRef` holds the text the server last accepted (set on load, and after a successful draft save, complete, or an offline-queued draft). Autosave arms only when the text differs from it. A loaded completed answer therefore never auto-saves; editing it still requires "Edit Response", which is the user's own downgrade.
  - **Review follow-ups.** An offline "Complete Response" now sets the ref too (before, autosave queued a draft behind the queued completion), and `QuestionContainer` keys `QuestionDisplay` by question id so a stale in-flight save cannot write the previous question's text into the next question's ref. Each has a test that fails without it.
  - **Verified.** Four new tests fail on main (13, 14, 13 and 13 calls) and pass on the fix. In a real browser against real Mongo: a completed answer sat idle for 40s with 0 PUTs and status still `completed`; one edit produced exactly one draft PUT with 30s of idle silence after it; "Complete Response" then produced one more PUT with 30s of silence.
- **Edit TOC now engages the TOC optimistic lock (#750, P0.2)**: the page saved with `PUT /toc` and no `expected_version`, so the #341 lock in `_update_toc_internal` never ran and two editors silently overwrote each other. The page now keeps the `version` from `GET /toc` (`getToc`'s type gains it) and sends it as `expected_version`. A 409 shows "This table of contents changed elsewhere since you opened it" with a Reload button that refetches the TOC and its new version (unsaved edits are discarded, and the message says so). A book with no TOC yet (GET reports version 0, PUT treats it as 1) is saved unguarded, as before. Tests extend `edit-toc.status.test.tsx`: version sent, version 0 omitted, 409 message plus reload plus resave with the new version. Demo: two browser contexts on one book; the second save got 409 and Mongo kept the first.
- **A stale tab can no longer silently overwrite chapter text saved from another device (#759, P0.11, backend half)**:
  - **The bug.** `PATCH /books/{id}/chapters/{cid}/content` `$set` the chapter HTML with no precondition, so whichever device saved last won and the other device's edits vanished without a conflict.
  - **The contract.** `GET .../content` now returns a top-level `last_modified` token (also in `metadata` and in batch-content). The PATCH accepts an optional `expected_last_modified`. If the key is absent, the save is unconditional as before, so today's editor keeps working until #760. If it is present, including `null` for a chapter that has never been saved (freshly generated chapters have no `last_modified`), the save lands only if the stored value still matches. A 409 `detail` carries `current_last_modified` and `current_content`, so #760 can offer reload-or-overwrite without another GET (overwrite means resending with `current_last_modified`). A 200 returns the new `last_modified`, so autosaves can chain. Clients must echo the token verbatim.
  - **Atomic, not read-then-write.** `apply_chapter_content_update` puts the stored value in the update's own query filter (`$elemMatch`, nested for a subchapter) and in its arrayFilters, so a save committed between the handler's read and its write makes the write a no-match. The no-match then re-reads: a chapter that is gone gets a 404, one that is still there gets a 409.
  - **Mixed stored types.** The PATCH writes an ISO string, but the bulk-status endpoint and the tab migration write a BSON Date. The token renders both as an ISO string with a UTC offset, and the write matches the raw stored value. `last_modified` now moves on every content save, including `auto_update_metadata: false`, so every save invalidates the token.
  - **Verified.** On real Mongo: the same stale token twice gives 200 then 409. A real competing write injected between the read and the write gets a 409, and a chapter deleted in that window gets a 404. Five concurrent writers holding one token produce exactly one 200. Removing the database-level match fails the window test and the concurrency test, and treating `null` as "no precondition" fails the never-saved test. The demo ran the same sequence with curl against a local backend.
- **The chapter editor no longer saves one chapter's text into another (#756, P0.8)**:
  - **The bugs.** `TabContent` rendered one unkeyed `ChapterEditor` for every chapter. On a tab switch the pending autosave timer was re-armed with the new `chapterId` while the editor still held the old chapter's text, so if chapter B loaded slowly, chapter A's text was saved as B. Separately, the content load had no ignore guard, so a late response for a chapter the user had already left landed in the editor, and the next save wrote it under the current chapter. Reproduced in a real browser on main, with only chapter B's GET delayed by 6s: B's stored content became A's text, and in the second case C's became B's.
  - **The fix.** `<ChapterEditor key={activeChapterId}>`, plus an `ignore` flag in the load effect that drops a superseded response, its error and its spinner reset.
  - **Found during the demo and fixed here: autosave died if the user paused before typing.** TipTap v3's `setContent` on load raises an update, which set `autoSavePending` with no edit made. When the debounce fired, the content matched the saved copy, so the timer returned early and left the flag set. Typing only re-arms the timer by flipping that flag false to true, so on main, opening a chapter, reading for more than 3s and then typing never autosaved. The no-op branch now clears the flag. The bug predates keying, but keying puts it on the main path: every tab switch is now a fresh load.
  - **Verified.** Four jest tests run the real TipTap editor (it is mocked globally in `jest.setup.ts`), and each fails on main. Mutations dropping the key, each of the three ignore checks, or the autosave flag reset are each caught. The browser demo stored the right text in Mongo for every chapter in all three scenarios.
  - **Left for #757:** text typed within the 3s debounce before a tab switch is not saved. On main it was not saved to its own chapter either: it was written into the next chapter. Flushing on unmount is #757's first criterion.

- **generate-toc no longer overwrites the stored TOC and every chapter draft (#753, P0.5)**:
  - **The bug.** `generate_table_of_contents` `$set` the freshly generated `table_of_contents` before the user had seen it, with no version filter and no check for existing content. The summary page always continues to the wizard, so editing a summary after writing chapters and walking the wizard erased every draft, and an autosave landing during the AI call was lost too. Reproduced against real Mongo: the stored TOC was replaced by the stubbed AI's chapters with the draft `content` gone.
  - **The fix.** Generation now only proposes. The wizard already called `PUT /toc` on Accept, so that is now the single writer. The response adds `base_version` (the stored version read before the AI call, defaulting to 1 as PUT does) and `replaces_drafts` (stored chapters or subchapters with content, counted with #749's `_walk_toc`). The wizard sends `expected_version = base_version` on accept, so a TOC changed during review is a 409 from the existing optimistic lock instead of a silent overwrite.
  - **UI.** `TocReview` shows an alert, "Accepting this replaces your current table of contents", naming how many chapters with written content would be removed, and saying nothing has changed yet. The wizard's review step now renders the save error; before, a failed accept (now including that 409) kept the button but showed nothing.
  - **Tests rewritten.** `test_regenerate_increments_version_not_reset_to_1` and two "generate-toc persisted a `generated` TOC" assertions encoded the bug. Both system-E2E scripts now accept via `PUT /toc`, as the wizard does.
- **Edit TOC no longer crashes on a drafted chapter, and "Mark In Progress" no longer 422s (#861, P0.1.1)**: the frontend's chapter-tabs `ChapterStatus.IN_PROGRESS` was `'in_progress'`; the backend enum, the autosave write and `types/book.ts` use `'in-progress'`. `ChapterStatusIndicator` looked up an undefined config and threw, so the error boundary replaced the Edit TOC page for any chapter past 100 words; the tab context menu sent the wrong value to `bulk-status`. There is now one frontend enum (`types/chapter-tabs.ts` re-exports `types/book.ts`), `MobileChapterTabs` compares against it instead of string literals, and the indicator falls back to the draft config for an unknown status. New tests: indicator with `'bogus'`, the Edit TOC page with an `'in-progress'` chapter, `updateChapterStatus` sending `'in-progress'`, and a backend contract test that parses the frontend enum and replays every value against the real `bulk-status` endpoint.
- **One author's chapter text can no longer appear in another author's export (#751, P0.3)**:
  - **The bug.** `ExportService` held one `html2text.HTML2Text()` on the module singleton, and `_clean_html_content` called it from the export thread pool and from `book_stats` on the event loop. The parser keeps its state (`outtextlist`, `rawdata`) on the instance, so concurrent calls mixed documents, raised `AssertionError` from the HTML parser, and could leave the parser poisoned until restart.
  - **The fix.** `_clean_html_content` builds a new parser per call, as `_content_to_markdown` already did, and `self.h2t` and `__init__` are gone. Any later markdownify swap (#800) must keep per-call construction.
  - **Verified.** A new test runs 8 threads over 8 distinct tagged documents, 200 calls each, and asserts no output contains another document's tag. It fails on main with the parser `AssertionError` and passes 3 of 3 runs on the fix.

- **Saving the Edit TOC page no longer erases every chapter's draft (#749, P0.1)**:
  - **The bug.** `_update_toc_internal` merged the TOC only at the top level. The Edit TOC page sends each chapter as `{id, title, description, level, order, subchapters}`, so its `chapters` list replaced the stored one. One rename set every chapter's and subchapter's `content`, `status` and `word_count` to `None`, and the API answered 200. Reproduced on real Mongo: 3 of the 4 new tests fail on main.
  - **The fix.** `_carry_server_fields` now indexes the stored tree by id, with chapters and subchapters flattened so a subchapter moved under another parent keeps its draft. It copies the server-owned fields (`content`, `status`, `word_count`, `last_modified`, `estimated_reading_time`, `is_active_tab`, `created_at`) onto the matching incoming items. Stored values win over client values. Ids the server has not seen keep what was sent, because the AI wizard computes those fields for new chapters. Omitted chapters are still removed.
  - **Malformed `subchapters` now gets a 400.** Before, a `null` value raised a TypeError (500), and a stored `null` would crash every reader that iterates subchapters. The walk also skips malformed *stored* subchapters, because generate-toc saves AI JSON verbatim. Without that, every edit of such a book would have 500ed; the glm-review bot caught it.
  - **Verified.** Mutations dropping the carry, the subchapter walk, the stored-wins rule, the id guards and the new validation are each caught. The demo drove the real Edit TOC page: it renamed a chapter, clicked Save, and Mongo kept all four drafts.
  - **#861 ([P0.1.1]), found during the demo:** the page crashes on main for any chapter whose status is `"in-progress"`. The backend writes that value after 100 words, but the frontend's chapter-tabs enum uses `'in_progress'`.

- **glm-review re-pinned: a superseded review no longer blames a timeout (#746, P3.47)**: the callee pin moves from `77dfa9c` to glm-review `main` (`9c7577a`). At the old pin, a review cancelled by `cancel-in-progress` (a newer push superseding it) posted "the step timeout, or a hung API call". Counting every September run: 33 of the 36 cancelled runs were supersessions, and all of them posted that timeout reason. The other 3 were 20-minute job timeouts under the pin before `77dfa9c`. The supersessions buried the **4 real 35-minute step timeouts** (#619, #622, #631, #636; runs 34299360608, 34403229853, 34425433212, 34439383436, all 2026-09-09/10, none since) in noise. #622 met #607's last criterion, so #607 closed. The new callee checks the cancelled outcome before the result subtype and says the run was superseded (glm-review#12). It also lowers `API_TIMEOUT_MS` from 50m to 20m, below the 35m step timeout, so a hung request like those four surfaces as an error rather than a silent kill. Permissions (`contents: read`, `pull-requests: write`) and the `ZHIPU_API_KEY` secret are unchanged, and the new inputs are optional.

### 2026-10-01

- **`nwsapi` override removed: 2.2.28 fixed the `:modal` recursion upstream (#589, P2.26)**: the `"nwsapi": "2.2.25"` pin in `frontend/package.json` `overrides` (#590) is gone, and the lockfile floats to 2.2.28 (published 2026-09-18 by the long-time maintainer, with npm registry signatures and no install scripts). Swapping only `nwsapi` on the same tree, `ChapterTab.keyboard.test.tsx` fails 16 of 23 on timeouts in 202s on 2.2.27, and passes 23/23 in 1.8s on 2.2.28 (both runs captured in the demo). 2.2.28's source diff contains the fix: the host matcher is captured once, so jsdom's `node.matches` can no longer re-enter nwsapi. The lockfile diff is that one package. That keyboard test remains the tripwire if a later nwsapi regresses.

- **Six new advisories patched, including a critical Next.js RCE (#738, P0.1)**: the `Security Audit` baseline gate began failing on advisories published after 2026-09-29. Not one of them was a reason to grow the baseline: `next` 16.3.4 → **16.3.6** (GHSA-vcvr-r3jv-pc5j, critical RCE in `next/og` `ImageResponse`; this app never imports `next/og`), `brace-expansion` floated to 1.1.21 / 2.1.7 / 5.0.12 inside its existing ranges (two high recursion DoS, reached only through eslint and `@sentry/nextjs` → glob), and `urllib3` 2.7.0 → 2.8.0 in `uv.lock` (PYSEC-2026-4175/4176/4177; five dependents: botocore, cloudinary, requests, sentry-sdk and, in the load-test extra, geventhttpclient). npm resolved `^16.3.6` to 16.3.8, published the day before. That is inside the 7-day patch cooldown this repo's `dependabot.yml` applies, so the lock takes 16.3.6, the oldest release that fixes the advisory. Dependabot's open frontend-prod group (#729) only reaches 16.3.5, which is still vulnerable. These landed in the same PR as #736 because both failed the one required check, and neither could go green alone.

- **`main` red since #732: security-update groups now obey the Dependabot guards (#736, P1.16)**: #732 grouped every ecosystem's advisory fixes into a `security-updates` group with `patterns: ["*"]` and nothing else, and merged with the required `Security Audit` check failing. Four guards in `scripts/test_dependabot_config.py` rejected it, so every PR after it inherited the red check: no `update-types` (production majors can batch, #507), a major-taking group matching `tailwindcss` (#555), and a group matching `better-auth` (#556's auth outage). The npm and uv security groups now take `update-types: ["minor", "patch"]` and the npm one excludes `better-auth`. The github-actions group still takes majors: that ecosystem is exempt from the major rule, as its version-update group is. Grouping stays, since that was the owner's decision. A security major or a better-auth fix matches no group, and per GitHub's options reference it opens as its own PR, still immediately. The guards were not loosened.

### 2026-09-14

- **Staging E2E account scoped, and the #550 throwaway accounts deleted (#603, P3.32)**: #599's last criterion, deferred there, was to make a *future* leak of the staging E2E credentials uninteresting rather than merely contained. The decision is written into the staging E2E README: the account is a dedicated, fixture-only staging user with no privileges a fresh signup lacks, staging holds no real users, and the residual risk is accepted on those grounds. No scheduled rotation; the manual #599 procedure stays, and the decision names the three conditions under which it expires. The demo doc for #550 recorded five `+aa550-*` throwaway accounts left in staging Atlas; an inventory found **six**, one with 2FA and one owning two books. All six were deleted from the box, with their sessions, accounts, 2FA rows, books and question data, after a script that asserts the expected counts before touching anything. Nothing in the app changed.

- **Book summary: which copy wins is now decided, which fixed four defects (#718, P3.46)**: the summary page had three writers of one field (localStorage draft restore, server load, the user) and no rule. **On the old page each of these was live:** typing during a slow load was overwritten by the server copy; the local draft was **never restored at all**, because the autosave effect ran on mount and wrote the still-empty summary over it first; after a `[bookId]` change the previous book's text stayed in the field and was written into the next book's draft key; and a previous book's slow load could land over the next book. **Decision:** the server copy is the source of truth and replaces the field when it loads, except over anything typed while it loaded; the local draft is the fallback when the load fails; and a book change starts from that book's state. A successful load already won on the old page, so that behaviour doesn't change. Implementation: edits go through one helper that marks the load as user-edited; the load ignores a superseded book and restores the draft in its failure path; autosave waits for the load to settle; and a book change clears the field during render. That removed the summary page's `set-state-in-effect` disable from #584. Five tests pin the rules: four fail on the old page and the server-wins case passes on both. Five mutations are caught; the stale-load one survived the first four tests, so the fifth was added. The existing "keeps auto-saving work under the gate" test now lets the load settle before advancing its debounce timer, and still proves text typed before then is saved. **Two pre-PR codex reviews each found a real P2, both reproduced by a test before fixing.** First, the loaded flag was a boolean reset in the effect cleanup, one render late, so switching from a loaded book let auto-save erase the next book's draft before its failed load fell back to it; the flag is now keyed by book. Second, a restored fallback draft read as unsaved and was posted to the server a second later with no edit, so a transient load failure could overwrite a newer server copy; the draft is now the saved baseline, so only a real edit saves. A third review found the same class of bug as the first: going A → B → A before B settled found A's first load still recorded, so the loaded marker is now keyed by visit rather than book. A fourth found that waiting for the load before auto-saving also held back edits made during a slow load, so a refresh mid-load lost them; auto-save now skips only the untouched field.

- **`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` is back at `error`, closing #584**: across #703–#712 and #716 the backlog went from 17 to 4, and four of those fixes exposed real races. The last four sites are effects doing work render cannot, and each now carries an `eslint-disable-next-line` with its reason on the line above. They are: the landing-page auth latch (better-auth sets `isPending` back to true on every refetch while `data` is null, so a derived value would flash the loader for signed-out visitors); the ChapterEditor → TipTap sync (content pushed into an imperative editor, with the saved marker moving alongside); the settings landing tab (read once from a URL the same effect rewrites; deriving it would break #221's tested clean URL); and the summary draft restore (it races the fetch and typing, and which should win is a product decision, filed as **#718** rather than made inside a lint change). The settings effect's three `setActiveTab` calls became one, which is equivalent since a checkout result already overrode `?tab=`. With the `"warn"` override gone, a new violation fails `npm run lint` in CI: removing one disable makes lint exit 1 with 1 error. The #675 ledger, its gate script, npm script and CI step are deleted, since the error-level rule enforces more than they did. `quality-standards.md` now states the bar for a new disable: name the external system or constraint, or derive the value instead.

- **Dashboard loads books from one keyed effect (#584, 5 → 4)**: `fetchBooks` set `isLoading(true)` synchronously and was called from an effect plus four handlers (create, delete, and two retry buttons). The load now runs inside the effect, keyed on the session, the page and a reload attempt. The handlers bump the attempt, and the effect's cleanup drops a superseded response. That replaces the request-id ref with the same guarantee: a page click, a delete refetch and a session change can still overlap, and the newest wins. `isLoading` is derived from which load settled, which also removes a one-frame not-loading flicker when a failed page change rolls the page back. Every handler call was a fire-and-forget reload: the delete handler's `await` had nothing after it and the loader never throws. The 63 dashboard tests (pagination, deletes, skeletons, accessibility) pass unchanged, and they catch mutations that stop dropping superseded responses (2 failed), derive loading without the key (3 failed), and make reload a no-op (6 failed). I had set this site aside as too entangled; the effect-local loader shape from #711 turned out to fit.

- **Every `jest.mock('@/lib/auth-client', factory)` silently missed the code under test, from #39 until now (#713, P3.45)**: the cause was a root manual mock, `frontend/__mocks__/@/lib/auth-client.ts`, left from the Clerk to better-auth migration. SWC compiles `import … from '@/lib/auth-client'` to `require('../lib/auth-client')`, following tsconfig paths, while `jest.mock` keeps the alias. Jest folded the manual mock into only the aliased specifier's module id, so factories registered under one id and components loaded the real module under the other. **Found by experiment, not by reading**: inside a single test file, a top-level `import` returned the real module while a later `require` of the same specifier returned the factory; printing the SWC output showed the rewrite, and listing every `__mocks__` directory turned up the file. Consequences: the 114-line global factory in `jest.setup.ts` never ran, and **fourteen test files' own factories never reached their components**. Those tests passed against the real module built on the `better-auth/react` mock. The root mock is deleted, which makes the fourteen factories live. The `jest.setup.ts` factory is deleted too: once live, it broke eight tests that had only ever run against the real module, since it lacks `twoFactor.generateBackupCodes` among others. With both gone the suite is unchanged at 2803 passed. `authClientMockReachesImports.test.ts` mocks the module and imports it through the same SWC rewrite; restoring the root mock fails it. #714's changelog entry calls the mechanism unknown and the block "left in place"; both are superseded here.

- **The test `useSession` returns one stable session, as better-auth does**: the `better-auth/react` mock built a new session object, with a fresh `expiresAt`, on every call. Components list `session` in effect and callback deps, so the book page refetched in a loop under it; #712 worked around that per test. The mock now returns one object, and the book page tests drop their workaround and become the tripwire: restoring a fresh object per call makes both time out. **This is the mock components actually get.** `@/lib/auth-client` builds its client from it through `moduleNameMapper`, and in this setup `jest.mock('@/lib/auth-client', factory)` does not take effect, neither the 114-line global one in `jest.setup.ts` nor a test file's own. An edit to the `jest.setup.ts` mock was tried first and changed nothing, and removing that block entirely leaves the suite at an identical 2803 passed. It was left in place, because the mechanism isn't understood and tests that believe they mock `authClient` methods through such a factory may not be; that is filed separately.

- **A book the user had left could render, and save, under the next one (#584, 6 → 5)**: the app router keeps the book page mounted across a `[bookId]` change, and its loader had no stale-response guard. With book A's request slow and book B's fast, A's details landed last and rendered under B's URL, where the metadata form would save A's values to B. A test reproduces it on the old page. The load now runs inside its effect and drops a response once the book, attempt or session has moved on; Try Again starts a new attempt; `isLoading` is derived from which load settled. A second test, passing on the old page, pins that Try Again shows the skeleton. **The page had no test file, and building one turned up a harness trap**: `@/lib/auth-client` is mocked globally in `jest.setup.ts`, a file-level `jest.mock` of it has no effect, and the global `useSession` returns a new session object, with a fresh `expiresAt`, on every call. This page lists `session` in its loader's deps, so under the default mock it refetched 73 times in 300 ms and every test timed out. The tests configure the global mock with one stable object, as better-auth's own `data` is. Mutations applying a left book's response, deriving loading without the key, and making Try Again a no-op are each caught.

- **Interview questions: a previous chapter's late response replaced the current chapter's (#584, 7 → 6)**: `QuestionContainer` is reused across chapters, and its initial load had no stale-response guard. With chapter 1's request slow and chapter 2's fast, chapter 1's questions landed last and replaced chapter 2's on screen. Answers are saved against the question ids shown, so this could attach answers to the wrong chapter's questions. The same applied to the progress fetch that follows. Both reproduce on the old component. The chapter's initial load now runs inside its effect, ignores a response and its progress once the chapter has changed, and records which chapter has settled; loading is derived from that. Shared apply and error helpers serve both it and the handler-driven reload (retry, refresh after generating or saving), which keeps its own flag. A chapter change clears the previous error during render, as each load used to when it began. **Two tests needed a second pass.** Mutations dropping the progress guard and the error reset survived the first cut; the progress test was then added. The first error test was vacuous: it looked for the generator's error while chapter 2 loaded, where the skeleton hides it whatever the state. The stale error is only visible while chapter 1's questions are still displayed, so the test now raises the banner through a failed refresh, then switches chapter. It passes on the old component, which confirms that behaviour is kept.

- **Chapter backup banner is read from storage, which fixed two ways it lied (#584, 8 → 7)**: `ChapterEditor`'s `hasBackup` was set by a mount effect and kept in step with `localStorage` by six handlers. **Two cases disagreed with storage, both user-visible.** First, the effect never reset on a chapter change, and TabContent reuses the editor without a key, so chapter A's "local backup available" banner showed on chapter B, where Restore found nothing. Second, a failed manual save set the flag even when the backup write threw, so the banner offered to restore nothing. The banner is now read through `useSyncExternalStore` for the chapter on screen, and the handlers bump a counter to re-read after a write (storage fires no event in the tab that wrote it). Validation parses a whole chapter, and this editor re-renders on every keystroke, so it reruns only when the stored string changes. Both cases have tests that fail on the old code, and the existing backup tests pass unchanged. Mutations skipping validation, never re-reading after a write, reusing a stale cache entry, and pinning the key to the first chapter are each caught. The two new tests use a typed mock helper instead of the bare `{ content }` the file's older tests pass, which the #625 test-typecheck ledger records as backlog; the first draft added two more such errors and the gate caught them.

- **Stored export defaults apply during render (#584, 9 → 8)**: `ExportOptionsModal` applied a new preferences object in an effect, so the modal first painted the old options. It now compares against the previous preferences object during render and applies the change there, the pattern from #704. #693's rule is unchanged: the first arrival defers to a choice the user already made, and every later arrival wins. "Touched" became state rather than a ref, because render may not read a ref (`react-hooks/refs`); the three handlers set it exactly where they set the ref. #693's four tests and `PreferenceDefaults` pass unchanged, and they catch mutations restoring the pre-#693 guard-every-arrival bug, never deferring, a handler that forgets to mark touched, and never recording the first arrival.

- **reset-password derives its URL error message (#584, 10 → 9), and a vacuous test was hiding it**: an effect copied the `?error=` code's message into the form's `error` state after the first render. With an error in the URL the form never renders, so that message is the only one the invalid-link card can show, and it is now a pure function of the URL. That also removes a one-render flash of the generic fallback. #584's table had called this "a UX decision" about splitting 14 `setError` call sites; none of those can run while the URL carries an error. **The expired-token test asserted `/invalid reset link/`, which the page title matches in every invalid state**, so it passed whatever the description said. It now asserts the expired message, and two new tests cover an unrecognised code (with a token present) and the no-error fallback; all pass against the old effect. Mutations describing an expired link as invalid, dropping the unknown-code message, and rendering without the URL are each caught; the first would have passed the old test.

- **Settings page derives its load state from the current attempt (#584, 11 → 10)**: the same shape as #706. `loadPreferences` set `'loading'` synchronously before each request, which only mattered on Retry. Retry now starts a new attempt, and an attempt with no recorded outcome is loading. The existing `active` guard and the once-per-visit theme-sync ref are unchanged. A test written first, against the old code, pins what Retry shows in flight: the disabled "Loading..." button and no error alert. Mutations ignoring the attempt key, making Retry a no-op, and dropping the error outcome are each caught. The file's other warning, the `?checkout=`/`?tab=` effect, stays ledgered: it strips the param it reads and fires a toast.

- **ActiveSessionsList derives its loading state from the current attempt (#584, 12 → 11)**: the loader set `'loading'` synchronously before each request, which only mattered on Retry, since the initial state is already `'loading'`. Retry now starts a new attempt, the effect records how an attempt ended, and an attempt with no outcome is loading, the same keyed shape as #705. Two tests pin the retry path first, against the old code: Retry shows the loading status and hides the error while in flight, and a retry that fails shows the error again. Mutations ignoring the attempt key, making Retry a no-op, and dropping the error outcome are each caught. **Deliberately no stale-response guard**, unlike #705: Retry only renders once the current attempt has failed, so two attempts can never be in flight, and a guard there would be untestable code.

- **Export modal book stats: a dismissed open's response could win (#584, 13 → 12)**: `ExportOptionsModal` set `loadingStats(true)` synchronously in an effect and cleared it when *any* response settled. Close the modal before its stats load, reopen, and the first open's response could show that open's figures and enable Export while the current request was still running. Landing last, it overwrote the current figures. Opens are now counted during render (the lint-clean pattern from #704), each open's request is keyed on the count, loading is **derived** (open, and no response settled for this key), and the effect sets state only after awaiting, ignoring a dismissed open's response. Reopening still hides the figures and disables Export until that open settles. **Five tests**: three pass against the old effect and pin what had to stay; the two race orderings fail on it. Two corrections came out of checking the tests. The first race test used a bare `setTimeout` to let the stale response land, and **passed against the racy version** because the render was never flushed; it now resolves inside `act`. And a mutation dropping the `ignore` flag survived that test, because keying alone hides a stale response that arrives *first*. The flag only matters for one that arrives *last*, which is the second test. The three `trackOperation` mocks returned a fresh function per render, unlike the real hook's `useCallback([])`, which would have made any effect depending on it re-run forever; they now return one function.

- **VoiceTextInput resets its mode during render, not in an effect (#584, 14 → 13)**: the effect copied the `mode` prop into state after paint, so a prop change first rendered the stale mode. It now uses React's documented pattern for state that resets on a prop change: keep the previous prop in state and adjust during render. **#584's table had this shape as blocked by `react-hooks/set-state-in-render` (at error here), and that was wrong**: probed against the installed rules, the guarded form passes and only an unguarded setState errors, with both probes run. Two tests were written first and pass against the old effect: a parent's prop change is followed, and a toggle holds until the prop changes, including a text → voice → text round trip. Mutations comparing against the shown mode, skipping the reset, and keying the reset on the prop's *value* are each caught; the value-keyed version fails only the round trip, which is what that test is for. `ChapterEditor`'s other "prop mirror" is not this shape: it pushes `initialContent` into the TipTap editor, which is effect work.

- **ChapterEditor reads its saved tab rather than copying it into state (#584, 15 → 14)**: the "init from storage" sites on #584 were filed as blocked because a `useState` initializer that reads `sessionStorage` mismatches the server HTML. `useSyncExternalStore` removes that blocker: its server snapshot is the writing view, React hydrates against it, then re-renders with the client's saved view. #674 had already used the same pattern for `useUserPreferences`. A choice made in the editor is tagged with its chapter key, which keeps the two behaviours the effect provided: reusing the editor for another chapter (TabContent renders it without a key) falls back to that chapter's saved view, and a choice sticks when `setItem` throws. **The hydration claim is tested, not assumed**: `ChapterEditor.hydration.test.tsx` renders to a server string, hydrates on a client with a saved view, and asserts no recoverable or mismatch errors. The naive initializer fails it with a hydration error. Mutations dropping the chapter tag, ignoring the in-editor choice, and never reading storage are each caught by the new toggle tests, which the existing storage-seeded ones could not reach.

- **Tailwind CSS v4 and tailwind-merge 3, with the app painting what it painted on v3 (#513, P3.24)**: `@tailwindcss/upgrade` did the mechanical renames and got a lot wrong, silently.
