Every commit printed `Backend Linting (ruff)...Passed` having linted nothing. **Mutation-confirmed both ways**: with an `F821` injected into a production file the new hook reports `Failed`, while the old `|| true` form exits **0 on the same defect**. **The rule set is pinned, which the issue did not ask for but the measurement demanded**: ruff 0.16.7's own defaults report **1522** findings in this tree where the documented classic default (`E4,E7,E9,F`) reports **218**, so without an explicit `select` the gate would change meaning on every ruff release and turn red on a dependency bump that altered nothing here. **Turning it on surfaced a genuine latent bug**: `ruff --fix` removed `apply_chapter_content_update` and `update_book_summary_atomic` from `app/db/database.py` as unused imports and immediately broke `books.py`, which imports both *from that facade* — they were part of the module's real public surface but missing from its declared `__all__`. The fix is the declaration, not an ignore. 218 → **0**: 153 safe auto-fixes, then the remainder by hand — two `F841`s that discarded the result of a side-effecting `await` (binding dropped, call kept), a bare `except:` swallowing `BaseException` around a `float()` parse, a duplicate `ObjectId` import, two dead availability-probe imports, an `if/else` whose first branch only bound a variable nothing read (condition inverted rather than left with an empty body), and a genuine `F821` — `tests/test_system_e2e.py` used `timezone` without importing it. `conftest.py`'s 13 `E402`s are exempted **with a reason**: it must set the asyncio event-loop policy before pytest and motor are imported. **Also enforced in CI**, since the hook is bypassable with `--no-verify` and only fires on `files: ^backend/`. Full suite **1259 passed / 9 skipped, 92% coverage** after the unsafe `E712` fixes, which is what validates them. **Two stale claims corrected**: `CLAUDE.md` said the installed `.git/hooks/pre-commit` was the bd flush hook running no quality gates — it is the pre-commit framework's own hook and the gates do run; and `black` is named as the backend formatter only in the *operator's global* CLAUDE.md, not this repo. No formatter is enforced and the distance is now recorded (`black` 36 files, `ruff format` 42) so the next person decides with numbers rather than adopting one blind.

- **The pip-audit gate scanned production only, so an advisory in a test extra could not fail CI (#522, P3.27)**: `uv export --no-dev` meant anything reachable solely through the `test`, `load` or `dev` extras was invisible to `scripts/audit_gate.py` — it could not fail the build and never reached `security-baseline.json` either, so it was not deferred with a reason, simply unseen. GitHub's alert list and CI then disagreed with no record of why, which is the exact failure the baselined design (#342) exists to prevent. The neighbouring lockfile-sync step already exported `--all-extras`; **the two steps scanning different sets was the real defect**. The issue offered widen-or-document; widening wins because the asymmetry was accidental rather than considered, and **it cost nothing**: the export goes 478 → 977 lines and the only advisory on the wider surface is click's `PYSEC-2026-2132`, already ledgered. Documented as well as widened — `docs/references/quality-standards.md` now states the scanned surface per ecosystem plus two limits previously discoverable only by reading the gate: **npm advisories below `high` never fail it** (`--min-severity` defaults to `high` and the workflow does not override it, so a moderate is not baselined, not reported and not a failure), and **only `frontend/package-lock.json` is scanned**. **The issue's driving instance turned out to be stale**: `GHSA-6w46-j5rx-g56g` affects pytest `< 9.0.3` and the pin has since moved to `9.1.1`, so both its alert rows now read `fixed` — verified against the live alert list rather than taken from the issue body, which still quoted `pytest==8.4.1`. Nothing to baseline as a result. Three mutations: a synthesised pytest advisory fed to the real gate **fails** (proving the widened surface is actually read, not merely exported), the true wider surface **passes** (no hidden backlog was being masked), and emptying the baseline's `pypi` section **fails** on click (proving the gate is not vacuous on this input). Incidentally confirmed #561's premise while measuring: `ruff` appears in neither export.

- **TOC updates replaced the stored document wholesale, so a partial save erased its metadata (#496, P3.23)**: `_update_toc_internal` built the new document as `{**toc_data, ...}`, so every key the caller omitted was dropped rather than carried over. `{"toc": {"chapters": [...]}}` is a valid body — and exactly what the E2E helpers send — and it silently cleared `total_chapters`, `estimated_pages` and `structure_notes`; `GET /toc` then answered `total_chapters: 0` alongside real chapters, an internally inconsistent response. Same root pattern as #492: a missing key resolving to a destructive default, here on the metadata rather than on `chapters`. Latent when filed — the only shipped caller, `bookClient.updateToc`, types all four fields as required and resends the whole object — so this is a trap for the next partial-update caller rather than a live failure. **Both remedies the issue offered were taken, because they cover different halves**: the document is now merged over the stored TOC so omitted fields survive, *and* `total_chapters` is derived from the chapter list rather than trusted from the client. The second is not redundant — every other producer in the codebase already computes it as `len(chapters)` (`ai_service`, `export_service`, the flat chapter listing, and the frontend's own edit-TOC page), so accepting a client-supplied value only ever created a way for the two to disagree. Merging cannot resurrect deleted chapters: the endpoint still rejects a body without `chapters` (#492/#495), and an intentional clear is `{"chapters": []}`, which is present and wins. **A third defect surfaced while testing the first two and is fixed here too**: the handler returned `"toc": toc_data` — the client's own request body — so a partial update answered 200 describing the partial object while the database held the merged one, and a client trusting that response disagreed with its own next GET. The response now reports what was stored, in the same shape `GET /toc` returns. Three tests, one per defect, each **mutation-checked independently**: reverting the merge fails only the metadata test, un-deriving `total_chapters` fails only the derivation test, and restoring the `toc_data` echo fails only the response-agreement test — so no test is standing in for another. Backend **1259 passed / 9 skipped, 92% coverage**; the TOC coverage file 32 passed and `test_toc_transactions.py` 55 passed with no regression.


- **Four stranded pale accents under AA, and a guard whose predicate the issue got wrong (#637, P2.35)**: the mirror of #632 — that banned a *dark surface* with no `dark:` prefix, this bans a *pale foreground* left stranded with nothing to swap it in the other theme. **The issue's proposed predicate could not be used and that is the main finding.** It asked for `(text|border|ring|fill|stroke)-<hue>-(200|300|400)` "with no `dark:` in its variant chain", which flags the light half of every correct `border-red-300 dark:border-red-700` pair — the idiom #631 established and #632 measured. On this tree that predicate returns 28 sites of which **16 are that exact correct pair**, so the four real defects would have arrived buried in four times their number of false positives, and "fixing" them would have meant unpicking #631. The guard tests for being **stranded** instead: no `dark:`-prefixed use of the *same property and same hue* on the line. That finds 12, with no false positives. It also skips comment lines — `ChapterTab.tsx` quotes `border-blue-400` inside a #629 explanatory comment, and matching prose would have put a ledger row on a file whose markup is clean. **Four live light-mode failures**, each measured on the surface it actually renders on rather than quoted: the summary page's input-error text (`text-red-400` on white, **2.77:1**), its revert link (`text-indigo-400`, **2.98:1**), the edit-TOC add-subchapter hover (same, 2.98), and the dashboard header's 32px book icon (same, 2.98) — that last one a *non-text* graphic, so 1.4.11's 3:1 applies and it missed by **0.02**, the kind of margin no eyeball review catches and that axe never reports at all, since its `color-contrast` rule does not evaluate non-text elements (the #623/#632 blind spot again). All four take #631's pair and land at 6.47–7.90 light / 4.5+ dark. **Eight sites are ledgered rather than converted, each with the measurement that justifies it**: `error.tsx`, `global-error.tsx` and `layout.tsx` are theme-fixed dark error boundaries — they render when the app has already failed, so they deliberately do not depend on the theme system, and `text-red-400` on their literal `bg-gray-900`/`950` is 6.41/7.28 with no light surface for a `dark:` pair to swap to; three `disabled:` button labels (indigo-400 on indigo-800 = 3.33, green-400 on green-800 = 4.09 ×2) are exempt under **WCAG 1.4.3's inactive-component clause**, with the separate legibility question left to #642; and `VoiceTextInput`'s success toast is a light-only card that is correct in light (6.81) and wrong in dark (**2.78**) — #633's shape, not this one, needing the whole card rather than its border. **Mutation M1 found a live hole in the first cut of the guard**: injecting a stranded `text-orange-400` into a row that already carried `dark:text-red-400` **passed**, because pairing was checked per-property and not per-hue — and a pale accent added to an alert row already full of correct pairs is the commonest way this defect arrives, so the guard was quietly useless exactly where it mattered most. Pairing is now property + hue, with M1's shape pinned as a fixture. Six mutations in total (unledgered file gains one; ledgered file exceeds its count; a fix reverted; the pattern broken; a burned-down row left stale; a net-zero hue swap inside a ledgered file), all RED, tree verified clean after each. `npm run typecheck` never saw the new guard — `tsconfig` excludes `**/__tests__/**` (#625) — and running `tsc` on it directly surfaced **two real `it.each` arity errors** the repo's own gate would have shipped.

### 2026-09-10

- **Five hand-rolled spinner rings under WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1, and the sweep that found them (#635, P2.34)**: split out of #629, whose guard reads named consts out of two chapter components and so could never reach the thirteen other `animate-spin` rings in the tree — the #620 shape a third time. **The issue said eleven sites, two failing; the sweep found fourteen and six.** Each difference is a distinct way a hand-audit misses something: it quoted `BookMetadataForm` against `--muted` (2.33) when that spinner's painted ancestor is `bg-background` (**2.54**); it called the three `border-white`-in-button rings "likely fine, unverified" when the regenerate button sets `disabled:bg-muted` at exactly the moment its spinner shows, making that ring white on `#f5f5f5` at **1.09:1** — the worst ratio in the whole subject area, in the half it declined to measure; and it missed `border-primary` (**2.85** on dark `--card`), which is the #634 trap again — `tailwind.config.js` pins `primary` to brand indigo and #610 overrode `.dark .text-primary` *only*, so `border-primary` does not flip. `QuestionDisplay`'s `text-blue-600` (2.93 dark) and the dead `ui/loading-spinner.tsx` (`border-gray-300`, 1.35) were not in the issue at all. The five live failures move to the icon idiom (`HugeiconsIcon` + `Loading03Icon`) with an explicit `text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400` — #629's pair, 4.74 worst case; `ui/loading-spinner.tsx` is deleted (zero importers, not even tests) and the existing #620 guard caught its stale `gray-literal-baseline.json` row on the first run, which is the whole point of leaving a tripwire when you remove a thing. `spinner-ring-contrast.test.ts` sweeps the whole tree, resolves colours the way the app does (config brand colours, then palette, then token), and asserts 3:1 on `--background`/`--muted`/`--card` per theme — surface-agnostic, because a ring that clears all three is safe wherever it is mounted and tracing parents goes stale on re-parenting. **Review found seven defects in the guard itself, four of them live false-greens**: a Prettier-wrapped multi-line `cn(...)` hid the colour on another line; two elements sharing a source line pooled their utilities so a sibling's passing colour could stand in for a failing ring; the const substitution matched single quotes only, one quote-style away from blinding the sweep to #629's own site; and a ternary's two branches collapsed to one. Plus two latent (the brand lookup regexed the config text and would have taken a nested `sidebar.primary`; the ledger counted only light-theme entries) and the biggest — **eleven spinners had no colour of their own at all**, inheriting `currentColor`, so they produced no sweep entry and the "every ring" claim was false for them; they now need an `inherits` row naming the utility and surface, which the guard measures. Mutation-checked eight ways. The `.dark .text-primary` selector regex was unbounded and matched `.text-primaryX` by prefix — fixed in this guard **and** in #634's, which shipped with the same latent false negative.

### 2026-09-09

- **The errored ChapterTab was a light-only `bg-red-50`, so its label was 1.05:1 in dark theme (#634, P2.33)**: the mirror of #632 — that was a dark translucent overlay with no `dark:` prefix, this is an **opaque light surface never given a dark variant**. `ChapterTab` painted `border-red-200 bg-red-50` while the text on top kept coming from theme tokens that *do* flip: `--foreground` 18.10 light / **1.05** dark, `--muted-foreground` 4.59 / **2.36**, and the active title 5.75 / **2.73**. 1.05:1 is not low contrast, it is near-white text on a near-white card — on exactly the tab a user needs to read. The active title was a third failing foreground the issue did not name: the title span overrides the row's colour with `text-primary`. **Which is not the `--primary` token** — the first draft of the guard priced it that way and published 16.39 / 1.15 for it; the CI reviewer caught that `text-primary` resolves to the theme-fixed brand indigo in `tailwind.config.js`, repainted indigo-400 in dark by #610's `.dark .text-primary` override, while the `--primary` oklch token feeds only the v4-only `@theme` block that Tailwind v3 ignores. The guard now reads both of those files, so removing #610's override fails it. (The reviewer's specific "verified false-green" scenario — an opaque `dark:bg-red-900` card staying green — did not reproduce: `--muted-foreground` at 3.88:1 fails that mutation under either version. The wrong colour and the wrong published figures were real.) Both state surfaces now use #631's idiom, named as constants so the guard reads them from the component: `ERROR_TAB` (`bg-red-50 dark:bg-red-900/20` + `border-red-700`), `UNSAVED_TAB` (the light-only `border-orange-200` sibling the issue asked to check in the same pass) and `ERROR_ICON`. **`ERROR_ICON` was found in review, not in the issue**: the alert glyph renders only when `chapter.error` is set, so it is painted on this very surface, and while the card stayed light-only `text-red-600` sat on `bg-red-50` in both themes at 4.41:1 — so **giving the card a dark variant is what would have pushed the glyph to 3.01:1**, clearing 1.4.11's 3:1 floor *by 0.01* with nothing pinning it. It is now 5.91 light / 5.25–6.70 dark. The guard, `src/__tests__/theme/tab-state-surface-contrast.test.ts`, is static for the #623/#632 reason (jsdom carries no stylesheet and the repo's axe runs never render an errored tab): it reads the three constants with vacuity counts and asserts per theme that every token foreground clears 4.5:1, the glyph clears 3:1, and each border stays visible (>1.2), **including the errored-*and*-unsaved combination**, where `twMerge` leaves an orange border on the red card. Structurally it bans the bug class itself — an unprefixed colour utility with no *resting-state* `dark:` sibling on the same property — with `hover:dark:` not counting, `border-t`/`border-l` treated as different properties, and an arbitrary value the resolver cannot price throwing rather than being dropped as theme-safe (all four were latent false-green holes the internal reviewer found in the first draft of the guard). Because the dark card is translucent it composites over the **parent** — `twMerge` drops the row's own `bg-muted`/`bg-background` against the unprefixed `bg-red-50`, which `codex` confirmed empirically during review — so every assertion takes the worst case over `--background`, `--card` and `--muted`. Mutation-checked: dropping each of the four `dark:` utilities fails the guard (13 / 1 / 1 / 4 cases). Verified in the running app: the pre-fix dark render shows the errored label as a blank ghost strip, the post-fix render is legible in both themes.

- **Theme-blind dark translucent overlays: 18 sites, 17 burned down, one ledgered (#632, P2.32)**: split out of #624 (PR #631), where converting the TOC wizard and export page onto theme tokens turned their surfaces light and stranded every accent painted on a `bg-<hue>-900/NN`. An unprefixed dark translucent overlay is by construction authored for a dark page; composited over a light surface it turns to mud and takes the light glyph on top with it. **Six of the eight failures #624 fixed were icon badges and axe reported zero on every one** — its `color-contrast` rule does not evaluate non-text elements — so the guard is static, not an axe run: `src/__tests__/theme/dark-translucent-overlay.test.ts` sweeps every shipped `.ts`/`.tsx` under `src/` for a colour utility on a non-neutral hue at 700/800/900 with an alpha suffix and no `dark:` anywhere in its variant chain, ledgering exceptions per-overlay in `dark-overlay-baseline.json` with #624's stale-row assertion. **The issue's counts were both wrong and the sweep is why we know**: the title said nineteen sites, its own file table summed to fourteen, and the sweep found **eighteen** — the table missed `ui/toaster.tsx` entirely because its four overlays carry a `group-[.toast]:` variant prefix that a hand-written grep for an *unprefixed* class cannot match. Those four are the one ledgered row, and deliberately so: the toaster hardcodes `theme="dark"` on the Sonner root and paints `bg-gray-900` in both app themes, so adding `dark:` there would delete the severity tint in light rather than fix it; the real fix is making the toast follow the app theme, which `gray-literal-baseline.json` already defers for the same file. The other 17 use #631's idiom (`bg-<hue>-50 dark:bg-<hue>-900/NN`, `text-<hue>-700 dark:text-<hue>-400`), measured per hue on all three surfaces in both themes. **The issue's proposed `border-<hue>-300 dark:border-<hue>-700` pair does not clear 3:1** (1.74:1 on its own card; 2.14–2.86 in dark) and is not held to it: every one of these surfaces is an alert carrying `role="alert"`, an icon and literal text, so colour is never the sole indicator and 1.4.11 does not attach — the border is held to a visibility floor instead, documented as a drift guard rather than a WCAG claim. `ErrorNotification` needed a full pass rather than a class swap, since its heading was `text-white` and its body `text-gray-300/400/500` on what is now a light card; its gray-ledger row is deleted, and two dark-only `className` overrides on shadcn `Button`s were **removed** rather than re-themed, because `outline` and `ghost` are already token-based. **Verified with real axe in Chromium on a temporary route rendering all five severities: 32 `color-contrast` violations in light before, 0 in both themes after** — `#ffffff` on `#e5d2d2` at 1.45:1 and `#d1d5db` on `#e5d2d2` at **1.01:1** (literally invisible) among them. Axe's own composited figures also validate the new `compositeOver()` helper against a browser: it reported `#210e0e` / 6.69 where the helper computes 6.70. The four ratios #624 measured are reproduced in the guard **over `--muted`, not `--background`** — over white the same pairs give 1.10/1.91/1.03/1.02, so the surface is recorded alongside every figure. Mutation-checked seven ways (unledgered file gains an overlay; ledgered file gains a new one; ledgered file exceeds its count; a burned-down row left stale; the pattern itself broken; a fixed site regressed; a ledger row losing its reason) — all RED, tree verified clean after each. Guard net is 700/800/900 only: a 400/500/600 translucent composites to a pale tint rather than mud, and `bg-black/50` modal scrims are legitimately theme-fixed.

- **ChapterTab's loading spinner: a 2.33:1 ring around a theme-fixed white core (#629, P2.31)**: the last indicator on the row #623 swept, and the one it deferred as a redesign rather than a colour swap. The hand-rolled `border-2 border-blue-400 border-t-transparent rounded-full animate-spin bg-white` div failed **WCAG 2.1 1.4.11** twice over: the ring — the spinner's only shape — was 2.54:1 on light `--background` and **2.33:1** on light `--muted`, both surfaces the tab is ever painted on; and `bg-white` had no dark counterpart, so it read as the surface colour in light (the spinner appeared as a hairline ring, not a disc) and as a bright white dot in dark that nothing else on the strip has. **The fill was deleted, not recoloured**: the indicator is now the icon spinner already used by `ChapterEditor`'s save indicator (`HugeiconsIcon` + `Loading03Icon`), which has no fill to be theme-blind about, with the row's blue "working" signal kept per theme as `text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400` (5.17 / 4.74 light, 7.79 / 5.95 dark). **The issue's own predicted figures were wrong** — it quoted 3.56 / 3.26 for light `blue-600`, which is Tailwind **v4**'s value; this repo is still on v3 (#513), where it is comfortably higher. Noted in a code comment so the next reader does not read the gap as drift. The #623 guard was extended rather than duplicated: `dotColor` → `indicatorColor`, resolving `bg-` **or** `text-` **or** `border-` utilities, because colour reaches these indicators as a fill, an icon stroke or a ring depending on which one it is — no existing `DOT_SOURCES` entry captures a `text-`/`border-` utility, so no entry changed how it resolves (57 → 62 cases, all from the new one). Mutation-checked both directions: restoring `border-blue-400` fails on `light theme is at least 3:1 on --muted`, and renaming the const trips the vacuity count (1 → 0). **Demoing it in the running app found worse than what was fixed**: the errored tab paints `bg-red-50` with no dark variant, putting its label at **1.05:1** — not low-contrast, unreadable — filed as #634; and sweeping the rest of the tree found `border-gray-400` and `border-blue-400` failing identically at two more spinner sites, against four `border-indigo-500` full-page loaders that clear the floor everywhere, filed as #635. Third-party review: opencode failed with a server-side error, `codex review` ran clean.
- **The gray ledger was 464 literals and nothing said which were real failures (#624, P2.30)**: #620 built the ledger and deferred the burn-down. Real axe-core in Chromium across 5 routes and all 6 TOC-wizard steps, both themes, before and after: **29 `color-contrast` violations → 0**, and the ledger went **34 files / 464 literals → 22 / 182** with **12 entries deleted, not recounted**. The measurement contradicted the counts: the six TOC-wizard steps — 139 literals, the largest block — produced almost *no* axe violations, because they paint self-consistent dark cards (`text-gray-300` on `bg-gray-900`) that stay internally legible on a white page. They were theme-blind, not low-contrast. The genuine failures were elsewhere and severe: the export and edit-TOC `<h1 class="text-gray-100">` at **1.1:1** (invisible), the edit-TOC "Tips" box at **1.2:1** (×5), `ChapterStatusIndicator` labels at **1.94:1** on the gray-800 TOC cards (×8), `ChapterBreadcrumb` at 2.53 (×2). **Converting the surfaces is what created new failures**, exactly as the issue warned: every accent that had been sitting on a dark box landed on a light one, and the post-conversion scan caught seven that had not existed before (`text-green-400` 1.59, `text-purple-400` 2.64, `text-red-400` 2.76, plus `text-gray-400` at 2.32 leaking in from `LoadingStateManager`/`ProgressIndicator`, which the wizard renders but which were not on the target list) — each given a light-mode partner with no dark value moved. Two accent failures were pre-existing and theme-independent: white on `bg-green-600` is **3.29:1** at four call sites (now green-700, 5.02) and `text-amber-400` was 1.53:1. **`--muted-foreground` has no headroom**, as #620 flagged: on `bg-indigo-50` it measured **4.49:1 in the browser** — under the floor by 0.01 — because the token renders `#6f6f6f`, not the `#6e6e6e` the arithmetic predicts; the tint is `indigo-50/50` at 4.77 instead. Mutation-checking the burn-down also found a **hole in the guard**: re-adding a ledger row for a file with zero literals passed silently, so the ledger could pre-authorise a literal rather than record one — against its own `_comment`. A stale-row assertion now fails any ledgered literal the file no longer contains, while partial shrink stays free. `bg-gray-750` turned out not to be a Tailwind shade at all and had never rendered anything.
- **Chapter indicator dots failed WCAG 2.1 1.4.11 in light mode (#623, P2.29)**: split out of #620. Status is encoded as a `rounded-full` dot whose colour is the only at-a-glance signal — the label lives in a tooltip and the tab's `aria-label` carries no status — so each dot is a **non-text UI component with a 3:1 floor**, which `axe-core`'s `color-contrast` rule does not check at all. The named failure was COMPLETED's `bg-green-500` at **2.28:1** on light `--background` and **2.09:1** on light `--muted`; it is now `bg-green-700 dark:bg-green-500` (5.02 / 4.60 light, unchanged 8.69 / 6.64 dark). **`green-600` was rejected**: 3.02:1 on light `--muted` is a pass by 0.02, one surface re-tune from failing again. Sweeping the rest of the indicator row rather than only the named element found **two more real failures**: `MobileChapterTabs` painted the DRAFT dot `bg-muted` — its own surface, **1.00:1, i.e. invisible** — which is the #618 defect never fixed in the mobile variant (now `bg-muted-foreground`); and the unsaved-changes dot at **2.57:1** (`bg-orange-500` → `bg-orange-600`, 3.26 light-muted). The `border-orange-700` it carries is a separate 3:1 obligation, not a substitute for the fill's. New guard `theme/status-dot-contrast.test.ts` (57 cases) **reads the dot classes out of the four declaration sites** and resolves them per theme — Tailwind literals against tailwind's own palette, `bg-<token>` utilities against `globals.css` — so it fails if either half of a pair is re-tuned instead of asserting a duplicated colour; the thin passes (`blue-500` 3.37 light-muted, `purple-500` 3.82 dark-muted) are swept by the same cases so they cannot drift under. **Per-file match counts** are asserted, so a restructured status config fails the suite loudly rather than going vacuous (the #620 lesson). Six mutations verified it, including re-tuning light `--muted` to `oklch(0.80)` (14 failures) and moving a dot out of its config literal (suite failed, 0 tests, exit 1). Typechecking the new test **required a temporary tsconfig** — `frontend/tsconfig.json` excludes `**/__tests__/**` (#625) — and that caught a real error `npm run typecheck` would never have seen. Not fixed: the `is_loading` spinner's `border-blue-400` at 2.33:1 light-muted with a hardcoded `bg-white` core, a redesign rather than a colour swap — filed as #629.
- **The #331 gray-literal guard was a hand-maintained path list (#620, P2.28)**: `theme/core-authoring-pages-tokens.test.ts` banned hardcoded `*-gray-N` Tailwind literals across an explicit four-entry `PAGES` array — which is exactly why #618 shipped a 1.46:1 label (`ChapterTab.tsx` was not on the list), and why **every child step of `TocGenerationWizard` was unguarded while the wizard shell itself was guarded** (139 literals across `TocReview`, `ClarifyingQuestions`, `NotReadyMessage`, `TocGenerating`, `ErrorDisplay`, `ReadinessChecker`). The guard now sweeps **every shipped `.ts`/`.tsx` under `src/`** (182 files) against `frontend/gray-literal-baseline.json`, the same ledger shape `security-baseline.json` uses for advisories: **34 files / 464 literals / 174 rows**, each with a reason. Adding a gray literal anywhere fails; burning one down does not. **Whole-tree, not the transitive import set** — an import walk is precisely the mechanism that can silently omit a file (the issue's own dynamic-import caveat), so covering everything retires that caveat instead of documenting around it. Test sources are explicitly out of scope: they aren't shipped UI, several legitimately assert on gray class names, and the guard's own doc comment carries `*-gray-N` literals, so scanning tests would self-match. **Pre-PR review (codex) found a swap loophole** in the first cut — a single total per file lets you drop one `text-gray-500` and add one `text-gray-300` for a net-zero count, the wrong-shade edit that shipped #618 — so the ledger records counts **per distinct literal**. Six mutations verified the guard: literal added to a previously-unguarded ledgered file, literal added to an unledgered file, a brand-new file nobody registered, the token fix reverted, the `src` walk broken (caught by an explicit vacuity assertion, `sources.length > 150`), and the net-zero swap. **Second #618 finding fixed too**: light `--muted-foreground` `#737373` on `--muted` `#f5f5f5` is **4.34:1** — under AA, and `text-muted-foreground` is used on 65 components. `oklch(0.556)` → `oklch(0.54)` (`#6e6e6e`, **4.68:1**), light theme only; the new `muted-foreground-contrast.test.ts` checks the token against background/card/muted/secondary/accent in **both** themes and pins the maths to axe-core's observed 4.34 figure. The browser-validated oklch→sRGB conversion moved from the #610 guard into `src/__tests__/theme/helpers/contrast.ts` so both guards share one implementation. **Known gap, unchanged by this PR**: `tsconfig` excludes `**/__tests__/**`, so `npm run typecheck` never sees any of these files — they were typechecked directly with an explicit `tsc` invocation instead.

### 2026-09-08
- **Inactive chapter tab title was 1.46:1 on the dark theme (#618, P1.2)**: `ChapterTab.tsx` styled the inactive tab title with a hardcoded `text-gray-700` while the tab's own surface is theme-aware (`bg-muted` inactive, `bg-background` active), so on the dark theme — which is `defaultTheme` — the label was effectively invisible in the core authoring flow. Two more hardcoded clusters in the same file went with it: the status dot's `border-gray-400 bg-white` and the close button's `border-gray-300 bg-gray-100 text-gray-700 focus:bg-gray-200 focus:text-gray-900`. **The issue's suggested `text-muted-foreground` was measured and rejected**: it clears the bar on dark (5.85:1) but `--muted-foreground` `#737373` on `--muted` `#f5f5f5` is **4.34:1** in the light theme, under the floor — so the inactive title is `text-foreground` (**14.49:1** dark, **18.15:1** light) with `hover:text-primary` restored as the hover affordance, safe on dark since #610. **A second, pre-existing bug surfaced during review**: `statusConfig[DRAFT].color` was `bg-muted`, the same token as the inactive tab's own surface, so the default-state dot was invisible against its own tab; the `bg-white` in the base class list never masked it because `twMerge` drops it for `config.color` in every branch (verified, not reasoned). Now `bg-muted-foreground`. **Verified with real axe-core 4.13.0 in Chrome**: 0 `color-contrast` violations and 0 incomplete on the tab strip in *both* themes, and the "before" number is empirical — setting `#374151` back on the live nodes reproduced 3 violations at **1.46:1**, restoring gave 0. **Root cause of the missed gate**: `theme/core-authoring-pages-tokens.test.ts` (#331) bans gray literals across a *hand-maintained* three-path list that did not include this file. It does now, mutation-checked. The list itself is the deeper defect — a transitive import walk from the core authoring routes found **80 files reachable, 14 still carrying 272 gray literals**, including every child step of `TocGenerationWizard` even though the wizard itself is on the list — filed as #620 (P2.28) along with the light-mode `--muted-foreground`/`--muted` token pair, rather than bundled into a P1 contrast fix.
- **Header nav link failed WCAG 2.1 AA contrast on the dark theme (#610, P2.27)**: `tailwind.config.js` declares `primary` a theme-fixed brand colour (`rgb(79, 70, 229)`, indigo-600) — the `--primary` var in `globals.css` only lives in the `@theme inline` block that Tailwind v3 ignores, so it never drove `text-primary`. The active header nav link therefore rendered indigo-600 on the dark `bg-card` (`oklch(0.205 0 0)` = `#171717`) at **2.85:1**, under the 4.5:1 floor for normal-weight text, on every authenticated page. **Fixed by overriding the text role only**, under `.dark`, to indigo-400: re-theming the shared token would also move `bg-primary`, whose pair is `text-primary-foreground: white`, and white on indigo-400 is 2.98:1 — one AA failure traded for another across every default `Button`. `hover:text-primary` is named as a second selector because Tailwind emits its own `.hover\:text-primary:hover` at equal specificity and later in the stylesheet, so the base override alone loses to it and the inactive links keep failing on hover. Base and `hover:` are the only variants of this colour in the tree (swept for `text-primary/<opacity>` and focus/group/data variants; the `focus:`/`selection:` hits are all `text-primary-foreground`, a distinct class token). **Verified with real axe-core in Chrome, not jsdom**: `/dashboard` 0 violations with 153 elements passing `color-contrast`, the reported element measuring **6.01:1**, `/dashboard/help` `/terms` `/privacy` all 0, light theme unchanged at 6.28:1, and the #493 pagination nav scanned for the first time (0 violations). **Two guards**: `theme/dark-primary-text-contrast.test.ts` recomputes the ratio from the values actually in `globals.css`, so it fails both on a weakened override and on a re-tuned dark surface token — jsdom carries no stylesheet, so a `jest-axe` `color-contrast` scan there is vacuous and the test says so; and the `4.1 Navigation Components` `it.skip` placeholder is filled with a structural axe scan of both navs in their real composition (`<DashboardLayout><Dashboard /></DashboardLayout>`). Four mutations checked, each reverted and re-asserted.
- **js-yaml and sharp advisories turned the required Security Audit check red on `main` (#616, P1.1)**: two high-severity advisories published against packages already in the tree blocked every merge in the repo; reproduced against pristine `origin/main` with no local changes. GHSA-2883-xcg3-v3hh (`js-yaml`) spans **both** major lines (`>=4.0.0 <4.3.2` and `>=3.0.0 <3.15.2`) and `overrides` already pinned js-yaml at exactly those two now-vulnerable versions from an earlier advisory — so the pins had to move rather than the transitive float forward, and bumping only `@^4` leaves the gate red on the v3 copy under `ts-jest`. GHSA-rgj7-g3m4-5g8c (`sharp`, CVSS 8.9, bundled libheif) arrives via `next@16.3.3`. Both have upstream fixes, so neither was added to `security-baseline.json`, which is for advisories with no upgrade path. **The lockfile must be regenerated with CI's npm**: this machine's npm 11.6.2 dropped the top-level `@emnapi/core`/`@emnapi/runtime` entries satisfying `@napi-rs/wasm-runtime`'s optional peers, and CI's npm 11.19.0 then failed `EUSAGE — Missing: @emnapi/runtime@1.11.3 from lock file`. `npm ci --dry-run` against a populated `node_modules` passes either way; only a clean-room `npm ci` under CI's npm discriminates, and it was checked against the known-bad lockfile to prove it does. A `codex review` raised this before CI did and was wrongly rebutted on that bad check — recorded in `tasks/lessons.md`. The `nwsapi` 2.2.25 pin (#589) survived the regeneration, verified via its `ChapterTab.keyboard` tripwire.

### 2026-09-07
- **Dashboard pages through the book list instead of dumping 100 cards (#493, P3.22)**: `GET /api/v1/books/` caps at 100 rows and the dashboard rendered every one of them with no pagination control, so a library past 100 books had an unreachable tail — `skip` existed on the endpoint, nothing in the UI drove it — and first paint meant ~195 KB and 100 card renders on the primary landing screen. **Offset paging, 24/page, and the Mongo query is untouched**: #493's own notes measured an `_id` tiebreaker as a blocking in-memory `SORT`, which is what cursor paging needs, so the existing `.sort("updated_at", -1).skip().limit()` shape keeps riding `owner_updated_idx` — `explain()` on the paged shape confirms `LIMIT -> PROJECTION -> FETCH -> SKIP -> IXSCAN`, `docsExamined: 24`. **No total count and no envelope**: the client over-fetches one row (`limit = PAGE_SIZE + 1`) and derives `hasMore` from whether it came back, avoiding both a second `count_documents` per page and a break of `List[BookResponse]` (four existing API tests assert the bare-array shape). Measured 38,443 -> 9,643 bytes per first paint. Two backend changes, both consequences of the UI now driving these inputs: `skip` capped at 100k (matching the rationale already documented on the admin users route — Mongo walks every skipped document), and the rate limit raised 20 -> 60/min, since a dashboard visit used to cost exactly one GET and a 21-page library now costs 21 just to browse forward. **Three cross-family review passes (GLM/opencode) were needed**, the first two each finding real defects: concurrent fetches were last-write-wins (two quick deletes suffice — the per-card delete buttons are not gated while loading), the 429 from per-page GETs surfaced as the full-screen error branch evicting the grid *and* the pager, `pageStateRef` synced only from a passive effect so two DELETE continuations in one microtask batch both read the pre-splice snapshot, routing 404 through the new "already loaded" branch turned "no books yet" into an error toasted on every window refocus, and the pager's real `disabled` dropped keyboard focus to `<body>` on every page change (now `aria-disabled` + an onClick guard). 19 mutations across four rounds, all caught but one documented defensive line. **Process note worth keeping**: the first mutation round was invalid — a stale `.git/index.lock` made every `git checkout` revert a silent no-op, so mutations accumulated and four "caught" results were contaminated. Verify the revert, not just the mutation.
- **Sign-in rate limiting buckets per client instead of globally (#602, P2.23)**: The issue filed this as *unconfirmed*, with "find out what actually reaches the container" as task one. It is real, and the mechanism is one step off from the guess. better-auth's `/sign-in*` rule is 3 per 10s and its window reopens only after 10s of *silence*; when `getIP()` cannot resolve a client it falls back to a single `no-trusted-ip|<path>` bucket shared by every unresolvable request. Its default reads `x-forwarded-for` only, and without `trustedProxies` a chain longer than one hop is unresolvable — the leftmost entry is attacker-controlled. **The trigger is nginx's `$proxy_add_x_forwarded_for`, which appends rather than sets.** So a browser sending no `XFF` resolves fine — which is why the container, up since 2026-08-28, has never logged better-auth's warning — while **any** request that arrives carrying its own `X-Forwarded-For` becomes two hops and drops into the shared bucket. That is reachable on demand by anyone who adds the header, and arrives unbidden for users behind a corporate proxy, VPN or carrier proxy. **Demonstrated on staging**: five sign-ins claiming `203.0.113.7` returned 401,401,401,**429,429**, after which one claiming `198.51.100.9` was **also 429** — two distinct claimed clients, one bucket — while a request with no `XFF` was unaffected at 401. **Fix**: `advanced.ipAddress.ipAddressHeaders: ["x-real-ip", "x-forwarded-for"]`. nginx sets `X-Real-IP` with `proxy_set_header` from `$remote_addr`, which *overwrites* a client-supplied value, so it is always one hop and unspoofable; the container is bound to `127.0.0.1:3002` and cannot be reached without that rewrite. **`trustedProxies` was rejected on the merits**, not skipped: nginx is the last hop and never appears in the forwarded chain, so any address listed there would never be matched — the setting would work only as a side effect of flipping the resolver into chain-walking mode, which is worse than not configuring it. `x-forwarded-for` stays second so deployments with no nginx keep today's behaviour. **Outcome evidence, before and after against the real handler**: attacker and victim on different real addresses, attacker burns their three — victim goes from **429 (locked out)** to **401 (unaffected)**. **AC4 recorded**: a second proxy in front of nginx would make `X-Real-IP` that proxy's address and re-collapse every user into one bucket; the caveat and its two exits are written next to the nginx step in `docs/STAGING-DEPLOYMENT.md`. **Guard** drives the real `getIP()` rather than asserting the config shape — the bug lived in what the library does with the default headers behind *this* nginx — and mutation-checking it back to `['x-forwarded-for']` fails 3 of 5. Enabling that test exposed a second, quieter thing: `transformIgnorePatterns` is OR'd and next/jest **prepends** its own `/node_modules/` entry, so `jest.config.cjs`'s custom `(?!(better-auth|@clerk))` pattern has never un-ignored anything — it was inert, and the better-auth mocks were carrying it. next's entry is patched now, and the patch throws a named error if next/jest changes that string instead of degrading to a cryptic ESM parse failure. **Deliberately not fixed**: the backend limiter's `request.client.host` fallback is behind an authenticated-user key and only reachable if an authenticated caller has no id — a dead branch, not a second instance. Review: `codex review --base main` — no findings; opencode/GLM produced zero bytes in 12 minutes and was abandoned per the known failure mode. Evidence: [`docs/demos/2026-09-07-issue-602-signin-rate-limit-per-client.md`](demos/2026-09-07-issue-602-signin-rate-limit-per-client.md).
- **GLM Review stops going red on bot PRs, and stops posting empty reviews (#519, P3.26; #547, P3.26.1)**: PR #605. Two failures in one workflow, fixed together. **#519:** `claude-code-action` refuses any run whose actor is not a `User`, so every Dependabot PR that cleared the size gate failed in ~8-15s with "Workflow initiated by non-human actor" — a permanent red next to four genuinely required checks, and `mergeStateStatus` `UNSTABLE` instead of `CLEAN` on every bot PR. The issue proposed `github.actor != 'dependabot[bot]'`; the PR instead tests `user.type == 'User'`, covering every bot rather than naming one. **Deliberately a skip rather than the action's `allowed_bots`**: opening that guard would not produce a review, because GitHub runs Dependabot-triggered `pull_request` workflows with a read-only token and the separate Dependabot secret store, so `ZHIPU_API_KEY` arrives empty — it would trade an 8-second bot-guard failure for a credential failure minutes in. **#547:** the old pin capped runs with a 20-minute *job* timeout, which cancels every remaining step including `if: always()` ones, so a killed review uploaded no transcript and left its "Reviewing…" stub standing — indistinguishable from a review that ran and found nothing. Re-pinned to a callee that puts the clock on the *step* (35m, 40m job backstop), makes `--max-turns 60` the limit that normally binds, uploads the transcript, and rewrites the stub into an explicit "⚠️ GLM review did not complete" notice naming the reason. `issues: write` and `id-token: write` were dropped with it — **not optional on the old pin** (GitHub fails a run whose caller grants less than the callee requests) but absent from the new one, which makes the caller block a hard ceiling: a future compromised pin cannot mint OIDC tokens or write issues. The in-file comment spells out that the pin and the permissions move together, because re-pinning backward without restoring them makes every run a `startup_failure`. **Cross-family review (opencode/GLM) found no Critical or live Major and one real latent gap, fixed before merge:** the action keys off `github.actor`, which is the PR *author* on `opened` but the **pusher** on `synchronize`, so author-gating alone would still go red on a human PR that a bot pushed to — and the size gate cannot save it, since `changed_files`/`additions` are cumulative PR totals. `sender.type == 'User'` was added to close it exactly; no workflow in this repo requests `contents: write` today, so the gap was latent rather than live. **Evidence:** all 8 Dependabot PRs #519 cites (#507-511, #405, #437, #438) flip from *ran → red* to *skips* when the new guard is evaluated against their real payloads, while #605 itself still runs — confirmed by GitHub rather than by replay, since its own `review / review` passed in 8m28s and posted a real review on the new pin. Callee verified at the pinned SHA (declares exactly `contents: read`/`pull-requests: write`; 35m/40m timeouts; transcript upload; failure notice), and the pin is *identical to* glm-review `main`. `actionlint` clean. **A correction on the way in:** most of the recent `cancelled` runs on feature branches were the callee's `cancel-in-progress` concurrency superseding pushes (179-842s), **not** #547 — only the three clustered at 20m20-40s match the issue's signature. Follow-ups filed as #607.

### 2026-09-06
- **Staging credentials stopped leaking into public CI artifacts, and rotated (#599, P1.1; #558, P2.25)**: This repo is public and `e2e-staging-tests.yml` uploads `test-results/` as an artifact anyone can download. **Artifacts are not secret-masked — GitHub masks `secrets.*` in job logs only.** Four things carried live credentials out: `error-context.md` (its page snapshot renders each input's *value*, so the password was in plaintext), `trace.zip` (request *headers*, i.e. `Cookie: __Secure-better-auth.session_token`, plus the password as a recorded `fill()` argument), the HTML report (inlines the first), and `staging-results.json` (the json reporter captures test stdout, and the auth fixture logged the account email — so `secrets.TEST_USER_EMAIL` went out verbatim). Found while investigating #551, which had made sign-in fail on *every* run, so the exposure was continuous rather than occasional. **Containment:** password rotated with `revokeOtherSessions: true` (old value now 401, every previously-captured session token dead), the `staging` env secret updated, and all **63** unexpired staging artifacts deleted — re-queried after, 0 remaining. **Mechanism:** `trace` is now `process.env.CI ? 'off' : 'retain-on-failure'` — off where artifacts are published, on locally where nothing is, so a real diagnostic path survives; the fixture no longer logs the email; all four files are excluded from the upload; and the duplicate `if: failure()` upload step is gone. Config setting and upload exclusions are deliberately redundant, so flipping one back on is not enough to re-open the leak. **The exclusions are demonstrated, not asserted** — the real `@actions/glob` 0.7.0 run over a fixture tree mirroring both include roots leaves only `screenshot.png`/`test-failed-1.png`/`video.webm`, and dropping one exclusion makes that file reappear from both roots. Two Majors in this work came from third-party review, both real: the `staging-results.json` email leak, and a README that told you to reproduce failures "locally, where traces stay available" while `trace` was `'off'` unconditionally — documentation for a diagnostic path that had been removed. **Also #558:** the local `.env.test` values are refreshed and now **single-quoted**, because `dotenv` treats an unquoted `#` as an inline comment — a 32-character password parsed down to 4 during this very rotation, and would have silently broken local runs. `.env.test.example` now documents where the live values come from and why quoting is mandatory; both the `#` and `$` traps are proven closed against bash `source` and dotenv. Three real personal email addresses were scrubbed from tracked files along the way.
- **Staging E2E "cold start" was better-auth's sign-in rate limit (#551, P2.22)**: The #83 session canary failed its first attempt on every staging run at a repeatable 32.4-32.7s and passed on retry. The issue guessed cold-path latency and then flagged its own contradiction: run 33030415162 was still slow 33 minutes after deploy. **It was never a cold start.** better-auth applies a built-in special rule to any `/sign-in*` path -- window 10s, max 3 per IP -- and `decideConsume` resets the counter only after 10s of *silence*, not on a fixed window boundary. The fixture signed in once per test: 11 sign-ins from one CI IP at 2-6s spacing. Replaying the login timestamps from run 34050880126 against that rule predicts the 429 on exactly the 8th sign-in, which is exactly where it landed; the failure artifact's page snapshot shows the sign-in form reading "Too many attempts. Please try again later", the frontend's mapping of a 429. The seven sign-ins *before* the failure took 0.9-1.1s each -- **including the first, 27 seconds into a fresh run** -- so there is no per-run first-auth cost at all and the 30s fixture budget was never the constraint. Fixed by signing in **once per worker** via Playwright's worker-scoped `storageState`: 11 sign-ins become 1. Verified `11 passed, 0 flaky` with a single sign-in per run, and 3.2m instead of 3.9m. Also: `failOnFlakyTests` under CI, so the canary can no longer fail, retry, and report the job green (retries stay for the trace); the sign-in page's own error text is now attached to a sign-in timeout, since the opaque `waitForURL` message is most of why this needed a dedicated investigation; and workers are capped at 3, because each worker signs in once at startup and a 4th would 429 its own bootstrap. **Pre-PR review caught a leak the fix would have introduced** -- the first commit wrote the worker's storage state into `project.outputDir`, which `e2e-staging-tests.yml` uploads whole as a public artifact, i.e. a live session token published every run; it now goes to a per-worker mkdtemp and is removed on teardown. That review also surfaced the pre-existing sibling leak filed as #599: Playwright's failure snapshots record input *values*, so the staging test password has been shipping in plaintext inside public CI artifacts on every run this bug caused.
- **The operator's own network scrubbed from the public repo, with a shape-based guard (#554, P2.20.2)**: Sibling to #544 (staging identifiers), different category — the maintainer's home connection rather than infrastructure. **The issue's five-location table was wrong in both directions**, so the scan was redone from scratch: eight distinct non-private IPv4-shaped strings across tracked files, classified by reading each line. **Over-reported twice** — `tasks/lessons.md` holds `1.2.3.4`, already the canonical placeholder (no change made), and the "client IP" in the Clerk webhook log resolves to AWS eu-west-1, i.e. **Clerk's own egress**, not the operator's connection (scrubbed anyway as valueless log noise, but the rationale corrected). **Under-reported once, and that is the one that mattered**: `INCIDENT-2025-10-19-firewall-lockout.md:47` carries a consumer **dynamic-DNS hostname** the table never listed. The issue's severity argument leans entirely on staleness — a 14-month-old dynamic IP is near-certainly reassigned, the former provider is retired — and **that argument does not apply to a DDNS hostname, which still resolves**. It is a live pointer to the connection it was set up for. Also scrubbed: the `/24` prefix in a `ufw allow ... port 22` line, the dynamic IP, and the ISP name in **both** places (`:46` and `:160`; the table listed one). Verified gone repo-wide by value, not just at the listed lines. **Deliberately not done:** 87 of the 88 references to the decommissioned host remain. `:6` was replaced as the issue asked, but that is cosmetic consistency within one document and **not a reduction in exposure** — scrubbing 1 of 88 is theatre, the honest options are all or none, and none is defensible for a dead machine at a retired provider whose references sit mostly in read-only `archive/`. Flagged on the issue as a separate decision rather than silently made. New guard `scripts/test_no_operator_network_identifiers.py` in `Security Audit`: three rules, each requiring an IP **plus** context (firewall grant naming a source, consumer dynamic-DNS hostname, public IP on a line calling it home/office/dynamic). **No rule names an operator value** — a denylist here would re-commit the strings it forbids — and fixtures use RFC 5737 addresses. The blanket public-IPv4 rule stays rejected with the sweep as evidence: it would fire on three SVG files and the 88 dead-host references. Mutation-checked six ways including two *must-not-fire* probes (a documentation address, SVG path data) and a vacuous-scan probe. Its own fixture test caught a real subtlety — `_is_public()` rejects RFC 5737 ranges, so shape-matching and routability are now pinned separately. Policy recorded in `docs/references/quality-standards.md` → *This repository is public*. Evidence: [`docs/demos/2026-09-06-issue-554-scrub-operator-network.md`](demos/2026-09-06-issue-554-scrub-operator-network.md).
- **Every GitHub Action pinned to an immutable commit SHA (#518, P2.20)**: 26 `uses:` references across the four active workflows, spanning 10 distinct actions, moved from floating tags to 40-character SHAs with a trailing `# vX.Y.Z`. `tailscale/github-action` and the `glm-review` reusable workflow were already pinned. **A tag is not a weaker pin, it is not a pin**: `actions/setup-node@v7.0.0` was as mutable as `@v7` — any tag can be force-moved upstream and every subsequent run executes the new code with no diff, no PR, no notification. `build-images.yml` is why this was P2: `docker/login-action` holds the registry token and `build-push-action` publishes the images `deploy-staging-containers.yml` pulls and runs on the box, so a repointed tag there reaches staging directly. **Each SHA verified in both directions** — tag → commit via `git/ref/tags`, then commit → tags to confirm the version comment. `codecov/codecov-action@v7` is an **annotated** tag whose `.object.sha` is the tag object rather than the commit; pinning that value would have produced a reference resolving to nothing, so it was dereferenced through `git/tags/<sha>`. That procedure is now written into `docs/references/quality-standards.md` → *GitHub Actions are pinned to commit SHAs* rather than left as folklore. Enforced by `scripts/test_actions_are_sha_pinned.py` in the `Security Audit` job (SHA-pinned, version comment present, YAML still parses), mutation-checked three ways with the guard staged first — including repointing it at an empty directory, since a guard that silently checks nothing reads as evidence. It found a real gap on its first run: `glm-review.yml` was pinned but carried no inline version anchor, and the comment was reordered to satisfy the rule rather than the rule loosened. AC4 (Dependabot keeps updating SHA pins) is left **open** — it needs the next weekly sweep and is documented behaviour, not an observation. Evidence: [`docs/demos/2026-09-06-issue-518-pin-actions-to-shas.md`](demos/2026-09-06-issue-518-pin-actions-to-shas.md).

### 2026-09-05
- **Four post-#517 Dependabot sweeps verified; npm's PR limit raised 5 → 10 (#539, P2.19.1)**: #517 predicted grouping's behaviour from a `dependabot/cli` harness; this closes the loop with observation — 24 real PRs across four weekly sweeps, 2026-08-26 → 2026-09-05. **The shape held every sweep**: three npm groups, one `uv` group, one `actions` group, every production major alone (`openai` 2.45.0 → 3.0.0, `pytest` 8.4.1 → 9.1.1, `pytest-cov` 6.2.1 → 7.1.0, `better-auth` 1.7.1 → 1.7.2). **Self-close audit clean**: nine PRs closed unmerged, eight of them supersession rerolls and one (#548) retired by our own `ignore` addition in #555; every `Updates x from A to B` line checked against `origin/main` and every bump present, the only exceptions being `eslint` (capped at 9 on purpose, #583) and `@typescript-eslint/*` (dropped as direct deps by #585). Zero false "up-to-date now" closes — the failure that motivated #517 did not recur. **`gh pr merge --auto` closed in production** by #540, #581 and #588 rather than by the arm/disarm test. Two corrections to the record: (1) the "no indirect residue" claim holds for *version* updates only — Dependabot **security** updates are advisory-driven, reach indirect deps, and can never be grouped (`applies-to` defaults to version-updates); three landed in ten days (`fast-uri` #570, `postcss-selector-parser` #578, `minimatch` #586). (2) `open-pull-requests-limit` **counts version updates only** — GitHub's options reference: *"security update pull requests are not subject to this limit and do not count toward it. There is no limit on the number of open pull requests for security updates."* So the raw 5-of-5 npm moment on 2026-09-04T22:28Z is not a cap hit: one of the five (#578) was advisory-driven. Peak *version-update* concurrency was **4 of 5** (#567 #572 #573 + `better-auth` #574) and **nothing was truncated**. npm's limit still goes to **10**, as headroom rather than a fix: the three groups are fixed overhead every sweep, leaving two slots for everything the config forces solo, and Dependabot never announces a PR it declined to open. `uv` and `github-actions` stay at 5, neither having exceeded 2. Evidence: [`docs/demos/2026-09-05-issue-539-dependabot-sweep-verification.md`](demos/2026-09-05-issue-539-dependabot-sweep-verification.md). No guard test for the new limit — `assert limit >= 10` restates the file it reads.
- **`nwsapi` pinned to 2.2.25; 2.2.26+ makes every `userEvent` keypress take 12s (#589, P2.26)**: Frontend test infrastructure. `ChapterTab.keyboard.test.tsx` went from **23 passed in 1.1s** to **16 failed in ~220s**, every failure `Exceeded timeout of 5000 ms`. **It presented as a Dependabot failure and is not one.** The suite broke on #588, whose ten updates include `@testing-library/user-event`, `@testing-library/react`, `@testing-library/jest-dom` 7 and `jest` 30.5 — all plausible, all innocent. Each was cleared *individually against `main`'s tree*, which matters: the first three bisection attempts reverted one suspect at a time while leaving the others at #588's versions, and all three still failed, which frames the innocent packages as guilty. Re-running the **green baseline first** (main's exact `package.json` + `package-lock.json` in the same worktree → 23 passed, 1.1s) and then adding packages *forward* found it in two runs. **Cause**: `nwsapi`, jsdom's CSS selector engine, reached as `jest-environment-jsdom → jsdom → nwsapi` on a `^2.2.x` range that nothing here pinned — so #588 caused this only by regenerating the lockfile and floating 2.2.24 → 2.2.27. Bisected on main's tree with **only** `nwsapi` changed: 2.2.24 and 2.2.25 pass in ~1s, 2.2.26 and 2.2.27 fail in ~219s. Profiling `Element.prototype.matches` across one keypress: **`:fullscreen` evaluated 29,999,829 times**, `:modal` 30,581 — each `:modal` triggering ~1000 nested `:fullscreen` walks. 2.2.26 rewrote the compiled-resolver cache from a `Map` to a hand-rolled ES5 LRU, but a repeated-`matches()` micro-benchmark shows the cache itself is fine, so the fault is the `:modal`/`:fullscreen` path. **Fix**: `"nwsapi": "2.2.25"` in the existing `overrides` block in `frontend/package.json` — 7 lockfile lines. **Verified both directions**: main + pin → 133 suites / 2288 passed / 36.8s; **#588's exact dependency set + pin → 133 suites / 2288 passed / 39.6s**, against 218s and failing without it. **No guard test** — `ChapterTab.keyboard.test.tsx` already is the tripwire, inside the required `Frontend Tests` check, and is what caught this. Removing the pin is gated on an upstream `nwsapi` fix, not yet reported to https://github.com/dperini/nwsapi.
- **ESLint 9 + native flat config; ESLint 10 capped as blocked upstream (#571, P3.29)**: Frontend lint tooling. `frontend/eslint.config.mjs` built its config with `FlatCompat` from `@eslint/eslintrc`, a package it imported but never declared — it resolved only as an `eslint` 8 transitive. ESLint 10 drops it, so `npm run lint` died at `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` before linting a file, and **every** `frontend-dev` group PR failed on it (#571, #580). **The one-line fix does not work and was measured, not assumed.** Declaring `@eslint/eslintrc` and re-running against #580's exact manifest gets past the import and into `TypeError: Converting circular structure to JSON` — `FlatCompat` cannot consume `eslint-config-next` 16, which no longer ships an eslintrc-style config at all, only flat exports (`./core-web-vitals`, `./typescript`, `./parser`). So the config had to migrate regardless. **Fix**: `eslint.config.mjs` imports `eslint-config-next/core-web-vitals` directly and spreads it. `FlatCompat` and the undeclared import are gone — #571 closes by deletion rather than by adding the dependency it named. Two knock-ons: the override blocks must repeat the `files` glob the Next config registers its plugins under (flat config resolves plugin namespaces per file, so a bare `rules` object fails with *could not find plugin "react"*), and `@typescript-eslint/{parser,eslint-plugin}` drop out of `devDependencies` entirely because `core-web-vitals` already bundles `typescript-eslint` 8. `eslint-config-next` 15 → 16 also ends a standing mismatch: `next` has been `^16.3.3` since #567. **ESLint 10 is blocked upstream, not here (#583)**: `eslint-config-next` 16 pulls `eslint-plugin-react@7.37.5` — the latest published — which peers `eslint ... || ^9.7` and still calls the `context.getFilename()` API ESLint 10 removed, crashing on the first React file. No version of the repo can fix that, so `eslint` is pinned `^9` and its semver-major joins `typescript` (#514) and `tailwindcss` (#513) in the dependabot `ignore` block, each entry naming the issue whose closure removes it. **Warned, not fixed (#584)**: `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` 7 adds three error-by-default rules that flag 30 pre-existing sites (`set-state-in-effect` ×26, `refs` ×2, `immutability` ×2); all three are downgraded to `warn` to keep this an ESLint upgrade rather than a React refactor. **Verified**: `npm ci` from a clean `node_modules`, then `lint` exit 0 (0 errors, 441 warnings), `typecheck` exit 0, `jest` 2288 passed / 5 skipped across 133 suites, `next build` exit 0, and `scripts/test_dependabot_config.py` 9 passed / 4 skipped. No new guard test — the eslint config's guard is `npm run lint` in the required `Frontend Tests` check, and the `ignore` entry restates config the existing suite already leaves untested for `typescript` and `tailwindcss`. **Leaves #580 unmergeable as filed**; it rerolls without the eslint major once this lands.

### 2026-08-27
- **`frontend-dev` no longer batches shipping build tools' majors (#555, P2.19.2)**: CI config + guard. #517's `frontend-dev` group exempted itself from the no-majors rule on the comment `# Dev deps never ship, so a major is a CI concern at worst.` That premise is false for build-time toolchains: `tailwindcss`, `postcss` and `autoprefixer` sit in `devDependencies` and generate the CSS bundle every user downloads, so `tailwindcss` 3 → 4 rode into **#548** as one line of a 14-update batch on the same footing as an eslint bump. `dependency-type` classifies a manifest section, not a blast radius. **Fix**: a new `frontend-build` group takes those three at minor/patch, and `frontend-dev` names them in `exclude-patterns`; their majors now arrive solo while their patches still batch. **The obvious wider fix was tried first and measured wrong.** Barring *every* group from grouping a major looked cleaner — one deleted condition — but reconstructing #548's manifest and running `npm install --package-lock-only` per package showed three of the five now-solo majors cannot install alone: `@typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin` 6 → 8 (peer `parser@^8.68.0`), `eslint` 8 → 10 (peer `eslint@"^7 || ^8"` from `parser@6.21.0`) and `eslint-config-next` 15 → 16 (peer `eslint@">=9.0.0"`). The eslint family is peer-coupled and only moves together; forcing it apart would have produced four permanently-unmergeable PRs — #555's own complaint, relocated rather than fixed. That measurement is why the branch carries the first approach and then replaces it. **Second half of the issue**: one blocked major took the whole group hostage, and Dependabot regenerates it broken every sweep. `typescript` (#514) and `tailwindcss` (#513) semver-major move to `ignore`, each entry naming the issue whose closure removes it. **AC5, measured**: #548 reconstructed under the new config keeps **9 of 14** updates and resolves **exit 0** — the whole eslint cluster crossing its major together — against `ERESOLVE` today. **Disclosed, not absorbed into "green"**: that resolution emits three new `npm warn ERESOLVE overriding peer dependency` lines (`main`'s manifest emits zero); warnings only, `npm ci` installs, but they belong to whoever reviews the regenerated #548. **AC4 to #514**: the blocker recorded there was `ts-jest@29` peering `typescript ">=4.3 <7"`; measured, `@typescript-eslint` 8.x peers `>=4.8.4 <6.1.0`, which is tighter and is the cap that actually fires. Swapping the Jest transform therefore does **not** unblock TS 7 on its own — #514 updated with the output. **Also corrected**: the issue said "twelve of the fourteen updates are unobjectionable"; measured against the manifest it is six (four range bumps plus two lockfile-only moves), and the config comment carries the measured figure. **Guard**: `test_build_time_toolchains_never_ride_a_major_taking_group` — any group that takes majors must not be able to match a shipping build tool. This is the case #555 asked the suite to grow, and it is a three-name denylist mirroring `_MUST_ARRIVE_ALONE` for `better-auth` rather than the dev carve-out being deleted wholesale. RED on `main`'s config, GREEN here, mutation-checked both ways (dropping `exclude-patterns` → 1 failed; letting `frontend-build` take majors → 2 failed). **Review**: `codex review --base main` — no actionable findings; opencode/GLM failed with an upstream `UnknownError` on two attempts. Demo: `docs/demos/2026-08-27-issue-555-dev-group-majors.md`.
- **better-auth 1.7 account-identity backfill; forgot-password route fixed (#556, P0.10)**: the mechanism #550 could not reach. 1.7 made `account.issuer` **required** and keys accounts on a unique `(issuer, accountId)`; `sign-in.mjs` filters on `issuer === "local:credential"` (`createLocalAccountIssuer("credential")`), so every row 1.6 wrote — which has no such field — stops matching and the handler logs `User not found` about a user it has already loaded. That is the whole one-way asymmetry: 1.6 never reads `issuer`. Upstream ships a backfill (1.7 upgrade guide, "Account identity is scoped by issuer"); MongoDB needs no DDL, so it is pure data. Adds `backend/app/scripts/migration_account_issuer.py` — **dry-run by default**, `--apply` writes, idempotent, exit `1` on refusal. It refuses rather than guesses on an unmigrated non-credential provider (a trusted issuer is a deployment decision) and on an `(issuer, accountId)` collision. Verified end to end against real better-auth 1.7.1 + real MongoDB: **401 → backfill → 200** on the same account row, `_id` unchanged (`docs/demos/2026-08-27-issue-556-better-auth-account-issuer-backfill.md`). **`main` stays undeployable until an operator runs it against Atlas** — gate documented in `docs/STAGING-DEPLOYMENT.md`, and note the runtime image has `/app/.venv` on PATH but no `uv`, so it is `python -m`, not `uv run`.
  - **New failure mode, not in the issue: password reset does not 401 — it duplicates the account.** `resetPassword` finds no credential account and *creates a second one* with the right issuer, so the user gets back in and nothing looks wrong, while the collection quietly accumulates two credential rows per rescued user that then collide on the new unique key. Every hour 1.7 serves pre-1.7 accounts makes more manual reconciliation. This is why the backfill runs **before** the deploy rather than as a repair after it.
  - **Second defect in the same upgrade: forgot-password was posting to a route 1.7 deleted.** `forgetPassword` → `requestPasswordReset`, `/forget-password` → `/request-password-reset`; probed against the real handler, 404 vs 200. So the one page a locked-out user would reach for was broken too. It shipped because the app declared better-auth's surface by hand — a `PasswordResetMethods` interface plus `(authClient as any).forgetPassword(...)`. Both blindfolds deleted; 1.7 types these methods itself, and `tsc` now reports `TS2551 Property 'forgetPassword' does not exist` if the name drifts (mutation-checked). CI already runs typecheck, so that is the standing guard. The unit mocks had stubbed the dead method, so they passed against something the library no longer has.
  - **Dependabot**: `exclude-patterns: ["better-auth"]` on the `frontend-prod` group, guarded across every npm group that could match it (`scripts/test_dependabot_config.py`). The group's minor/patch filter was never protection here — better-auth ships schema and credential-lookup changes under semver minor, and #543 buried this one in a 26-package diff.
  - Recording a miss: the first version of the migration suite compared everything against `LOCAL_CREDENTIAL_ISSUER` rather than the literal, so mutating it to `"credential"` passed all ten tests. The one value the fix turns on was untested. Pinned directly and cross-checked against `createLocalAccountIssuer` in the installed library.
