Accounts created *on* 1.7 work, so a smoke test that signs up a fresh user would have passed and shipped it. Proven by a three-point sequence on one untouched account — **200 on `sha-bae440a` → 401 on `sha-8c796a9` → 200 after rollback** — with the suite-scale corroboration either side: staging E2E green at 15:43Z (33089344716), **11 of 11 specs failing** at 15:53Z in the shared login fixture on first attempt and both retries (33090040109), back to `1 flaky, 10 passed` after rollback (33092235853). The incompatibility is **one-way**: 1.6 reads what 1.7 wrote, not the reverse, so this is 1.7's credential lookup missing existing records rather than an ordinary migration. `sha-8c796a9` was deployed rather than a newer tag on purpose — it keeps #543 as the only app-code change in `bae440a..8c796a9`. Staging ran 1.7 for ~26 minutes and is rolled back and green; no data lost. Also measured, since it was the one change typecheck *did* catch: `two-factor/enable` returns `['backupCodes','totpURI']` on 1.6 and `['backupCodes','method','totpURI']` on 1.7, so `main`'s 1.7-shaped `TwoFactorSetup.tsx` and the rolled-back 1.6 server are now a mismatched pair. Mechanism inside `@better-auth/mongodb-adapter` not established (needs live Atlas credentials); filed as **#556 (P0.10)**.
- **`backend/requirements.txt` retired, along with its regen tax (#534, P2.23)**: CI config + deletions. #521 deferred this call on purpose ("reversing that decision is a separate call"); #534 filed it, and gated it on evidence: *"Option 1 is the likely answer, but it turns on whether those two scripts are alive. Establish that first — do not delete on the assumption."* **Established by running them, not reading them.** `scripts/validate-test-environment.js` **exits 1 against a healthy tree** — all six "critical issues" it reports are its own staleness (it asserts `frontend/jest.config.js`, `src/e2e/interview-prompts.spec.ts`, `test-suite.yml`, `deploy-staging.yml`, `deploy-production.yml`, `axe-playwright`, none of which the repo has had for months). `scripts/run-test-suite.js` **also exits 1**: it shells to system `python -m pytest` and dies on `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'pymongo'` — the same suite passes **1236** tests under `uv run pytest`. Neither is wired into anything (root `package.json` has only `"test": "bash scripts/test-all.sh"`), and neither used the export in a load-bearing way — the validator greps it for three strings, and the runner's `pip install -r requirements.txt` sits inside a try/catch that downgrades failure to a WARNING. **So the "tooling that cannot read a lock" `DEPLOYMENT.md` gestured at does not exist**: Option 3 is factually excluded and Option 2 would automate a regen for a file with no reader. **Removed together**: the export, the `requirements.txt matches uv.lock` CI step, both dead scripts, their sections of `docs/testing/final-integration-guide.md`, the `exclude-paths` block added in #512 and the test pinning it. That takes with it the per-PR `uv export` tax (a five-at-a-time recurring cost — `open-pull-requests-limit: 5`), a whole CI step, and the duplicate advisory attribution from the dependency graph counting the export as a second manifest. **Contract reversal, recorded deliberately**: `test_uv_ecosystem_excludes_the_generated_requirements_export` asserted the exclusion this change removes, so it was co-deleted with its subject — not mutated to pass; the generic exclude-paths relativity guard stays and self-arms if an exclusion ever returns. **New tripwire, mutation-verified**: deleting the export removed *every* other check that would notice it coming back, so `test_the_generated_requirements_export_stays_deleted` pins its absence — RED when the file is recreated, GREEN when it is not. Without it, someone running the `uv export` command still quoted in older CHANGELOG entries would silently revive #512's unmergeable transitive-only PRs. **Also fixed rather than left broken**: six live setup instructions across `docs/testing/` told a human to `pip install -r requirements.txt`, which would now fail. **Review**: cross-family **opencode/GLM — APPROVE**, no Critical/Major. It verified independently rather than reading the diff — restored both scripts from the parent commit and ran them, reproducing the exact numbers (validator's 10 errors minus the 4 caused by the deletion = precisely the 6 stale assertions claimed), confirmed `pip-audit` re-exports from `uv.lock` rather than reading the committed file (`tests.yml:152`), and reasoned that #512 cannot recur while no manifest exists. Four of its six findings adopted: the absence tripwire, two stale comments citing the deleted file (`backend/Dockerfile:23`, `backend/pyproject.toml:72`), and this entry. **Known limitation**: inert `.tbd`/`.yml.disabled` workflows still say `pip install -r requirements.txt` — none execute, they already target the PM2 path retired in #484, and **#520** owns retiring them.

### 2026-08-26
- **mongodb 7.5.0 verified against staging Atlas (#516, P2.18)**: verification, no code change. #507 bumped `mongodb` **6.21.0 -> 7.5.0** in `/frontend`, the only runtime dependency in the auth path from that batch. CI's E2E runs against a local/ephemeral Mongo, so the residual gap was real Atlas connectivity from the box — TLS negotiation, connection-string parsing (`mongodb-connection-string-url` goes to a new major; the deployed lockfile resolves 7.0.2), and driver-side retry against a hosted cluster. **Done as a controlled before/after**, which required first restoring staging on the *pre-bump* build (#537) and proving it green: with staging down six days, a post-deploy failure could not otherwise have been separated from residual outage damage. Baseline `sha-de87eea` / mongodb 6.21.0: `/health` 200, sign-in 401, staging E2E **green**. Then `sha-bae440a` / mongodb **7.5.0**: `/health` 200 `{"mongodb":"ok"}`, sign-in 401, staging E2E **green** (including `regressions.spec.ts`, whose #83 session/401 and #54 answer-persistence specs exercise session handling). **Disclosed rather than absorbed into "green"**: both runs report `1 flaky, 10 passed`, and the flake is the #83 session/401 canary itself — first attempt times out in the login fixture, retry passes. It reproduces *identically* on the pre-bump driver (32.7s/3.9s on 6.21.0 vs 32.4s/3.7s on 7.5.0), which is exactly what the baseline was built to settle: driver-independent and pre-existing, filed as **#551** after a third run made it 3/3. **AC5 evidence is the sharpest**: the frontend container's entire log since startup is 7 lines, containing `MongoDB client connected for better-auth (attempt 1/3)` — connected on the *first* try with no retries, which is direct evidence on **two of the three** things #516 named as unverified (connection-string parsing, TLS negotiation). The third — **driver-side retry/reconnect** — is explicitly *not* discharged, and connecting first-try is why: exercising SDAM failover needs induced failure, which this pass did not attempt. The log snapshot also predates the E2E run, so it covers startup plus the bogus-creds probe, not sustained authenticated traffic. Driver version read from inside the running container (`7.5.0`) rather than inferred from the image tag. **Deliberate choice of tag**: deployed `sha-bae440a` rather than the newer `sha-8c796a9`, because the latter carries #543's **better-auth 1.6 -> 1.7** — a second auth-path change that would have destroyed attribution. **Scope**: the deploy carried 14 commits (`de87eea..bae440a`), 8 of them app-code rather than CI/docs-only, so the green result validates that bundle; only `mongodb` touches auth, which is what this issue set out to clear. **Not covered**: better-auth 1.7, now on `main` unverified against Atlas — same class of risk, needs its own pass, filed as **#550**. Demo: `docs/demos/2026-08-26-issue-516-mongodb-7-staging-verification.md`.

- **Dependabot batches are grouped, and auto-merge is enabled (#517, P2.19)**: CI config + repo settings. With no `groups:` every package got its own PR, and because `main` branch protection sets `required_status_checks.strict: true`, each merge flipped every other open PR to `BEHIND` — forcing a serial update-branch -> full-CI -> merge loop. The 2026-08-25 sweep cost roughly **2h20m and 9 full CI runs** to land what amounted to four one-line workflow edits, two version pins and three lockfile bumps. Worse, it was **lossy**: with four PRs racing on `.github/workflows/build-images.yml`, Dependabot closed **#504** two minutes after #498 merged, claiming `docker/setup-buildx-action` was "up-to-date now" while `main` still pinned `@v3`. It deletes the branch on close, so `gh pr reopen` failed outright and the bump had to be recreated by hand as #515 — and a self-closed PR is indistinguishable from an intentionally-declined one, so nothing surfaced the loss. **Fix**: `groups:` on all three ecosystems, with a deliberate asymmetry — `github-actions` groups **everything including majors** (its bumps are one-line tag moves where `v3` -> `v4` is the ordinary case, and it is the ecosystem the storm came from), while `npm` and `uv` group minor/patch only so that anything shipping a **major** falls through to its own reviewable, revertable PR. `npm` additionally splits dev from prod, since dev deps never reach a user. **Measured on the first live sweep** (2026-08-26, #539): **10 open ungrouped PRs -> 5** — an `actions` group (#540), a `backend` group of 10 (#541), a `frontend-prod` group of 26 (#543), plus `openai` 2.45.0 -> 3.0.0 (#542) and `pytest` 8.4.1 -> 9.1.1 (#527) each solo. Two groups absorbed 36 updates and both majors arrived alone. **Correction:** a pre-merge `dependabot/cli` run predicted larger numbers (`uv` 13->4, `npm` >=33->6); it ran with `allowed-updates: all` (so it counted indirect deps production never opens PRs for) and had no memory of previously-closed PRs (so it re-proposed `tailwind-merge` 2->3, declined as #510). The A/B was still valid — both runs differed only in `dependency-groups` — but the absolute magnitudes did not transfer, and the live figures supersede them. `openai` 2 -> 3 is the load-bearing result: a production runtime major arriving alone, which is the treatment `mongodb` 6 -> 7 (#507) got only by luck of being its batch's sole major. **The predicted "indirect residue" turned out not to exist**: `dependency-type` classifies *direct* dependencies only, but Dependabot only opens PRs for direct npm dependencies anyway, so the extra solo PRs the harness showed were its own `allowed-updates: all` artefact. The live sweep produced none, and no catch-all group is needed. **Also**: repo-wide auto-merge enabled (`allow_auto_merge`), verified by `gh pr merge 529 --auto --squash` succeeding where it previously errored `GraphQL: Auto merge is not allowed for this repository`; armed on a PR whose required `Security Audit` check was already failing so it could not fire, then disarmed. **Rejected**: GitHub merge queue — the issue listed it as "consider", and grouping removes the rebase storm at its source rather than managing one. **Tests**: `scripts/test_dependabot_config.py` gains two guards, mutation-checked — every ecosystem must carry `groups`, and no group reachable by a production dependency may omit or widen `update-types` back to majors (an *absent* key means "all types", which is the silent way this protection would be lost). **Known limitation**: AC3 asks for a live batch landing with nothing self-closed; all three schedules are `weekly`, so that lands on the next sweep. Demo: `docs/demos/2026-08-26-issue-517-dependabot-grouping.md`. Documented in `docs/references/quality-standards.md` (new **Dependency Batches** section, including the post-batch self-close audit).

### 2026-08-25
- **Dependabot no longer opens unmergeable no-op PRs for backend transitive deps (#512, P2.17)**: CI config. `backend/requirements.txt` is a generated export of `uv.lock`, and Dependabot's `uv` ecosystem parsed it as a **manifest**. Transitive dependencies exist *only* in the export, so a transitive bump produced a PR that edited the export alone — verified across the 2026-08-25 sweep: #499 (flask-cors), #503 (tqdm), #505 (pydantic-core) each touched `backend/requirements.txt` and nothing else, while the direct-dep PRs #500 (pytest-asyncio) and #502 (pydantic-settings) touched `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` **and** the export and merged green. The export-only PRs changed nothing installed (`uv sync` resolves from the lock) and failed the lock/export sync gate, so all three were closed. Upstream bug: `dependabot-core#13912`. **Fix**: `exclude-paths: ["requirements.txt"]` on the `uv` entry — the update job now sees `pyproject.toml`/`uv.lock` only, so a transitive bump is either a real lockfile bump or no PR at all. (Scope: this hides the export from update *scans*, not from the dependency graph — alerts still attribute advisories to it, visible today as one `pytest` advisory reported against both the lock and the export.) That matters beyond noise: an advisory landing on a backend transitive previously produced a *structurally unmergeable* PR on the security-patching path. **Deliberate trade-off, not automated away**: a direct-dep PR now leaves the export stale and the sync gate asks for one `uv export` command on the branch. A bot that pushed it would be worse — GitHub's recursion guard means a `GITHUB_TOKEN` push creates no new workflow run, so every backend Dependabot PR would sit with its required checks un-run on the new head, needing a manual re-run to unblock. **Also considered and rejected**: deleting the export outright (nothing in CI, the Dockerfile or the deploy installs from it) — #439 and `DEPLOYMENT.md` keep it deliberately for tooling that cannot read a lock, and `scripts/run-test-suite.js` / `scripts/validate-test-environment.js` read it; reversing that is a separate call. **Tests**: `scripts/test_dependabot_config.py` — pins the exclusion, and asserts every `exclude-paths` glob resolves to a real file under its entry's `directory`, which guards the one way this fix breaks silently (patterns are directory-relative, so a repo-relative `backend/requirements.txt` would match nothing and Dependabot would carry on unchanged). The `Security Audit` step now runs `pytest scripts/` rather than the one audit-gate file, so new script tests are picked up. Documented in `docs/references/quality-standards.md` (regen command verified locally to reproduce the committed export byte-for-byte, and the manual transitive-bump procedure). **Known limitation**: AC 4 asks for confirmation from a live Dependabot run; the `uv` schedule is weekly, so that lands on the next sweep.

### 2026-08-13
- **TOC save no longer accepts a payload that destroys the TOC (#492, P2.16)**: `PUT /books/{id}/toc` took an untyped `dict` body and defaulted both `toc` and `toc.chapters` when absent. **The issue left open whether this overwrites real work; it does** — `_update_toc_internal` writes `{**toc_data, ...}` as the whole `table_of_contents`, so a wrong-shaped body wiped the author's saved chapters, bumped the version so the optimistic lock recorded it as a legitimate edit, and returned **200 `success: true`**. Demonstrated end-to-end against real MongoDB: a book with 3 saved chapters, PUT the flat payload from the issue, read back **0 chapters** on the old code and **3 chapters, version unchanged** on the new. Both keys are now **required** (400, with `detail` naming the missing one); `{"toc": {"chapters": []}}` remains a valid deliberate clear, pinned by a test so the guard can't be over-tightened later. **Deliberately not a Pydantic model** (the issue's alternative suggestion): FastAPI would return 422 where this endpoint returns 400 for four already-tested malformed shapes, a public error-contract change wider than the bug, and the frontend branches on status. **`docs/api-toc-endpoints.md` documented the unwrapped body** — i.e. the destructive shape — so any integrator following it would have hit this; corrected, along with the endpoint's real 400 `detail` strings and its 409. Three TDD tests, the primary one asserting the status **and** the read-back. Backend 1236 passed / 9 skipped, 92% coverage. Review: opencode (GLM) pre-PR, no Critical/Major; its one Minor latent finding — the same wholesale-replace pattern resetting `total_chapters`/`estimated_pages`/`structure_notes` on a partial save — is filed as **#496 (P3.23)** rather than widened into this PR, since no shipped client sends a partial body.

### 2026-07-27
- **Supply-chain automation re-aimed: Dependabot at `/frontend`, baselined audit gate, dead crypto deps removed (#342, P2.1, security)**: CI + backend deps. **Premise re-verified before starting** (the issue's own comment had already corrected two sub-claims, and one more was wrong): Dependabot's npm ecosystem targeted `/`, where `package.json` is a PM2 shell with **0 dependencies and no lockfile**, so it watched nothing while `frontend/package-lock.json`'s **1122 packages** went unmonitored — confirmed, and the monitored surface goes 0 → 1122. The claimed "stray **untracked** `frontend/scripts/package-lock.json`" **does not exist** and both files there were **tracked**; the real finding is worse — `system-test.js` authenticates with `Authorization: Bearer ${API_TOKEN}` while the backend replaced JWT/bearer auth with better-auth session **cookies** (`better_auth_session.py` reads cookies only), so it **cannot succeed against the current API**. Its documenting `SYSTEM_TESTS.md` was equally dead: the other two entry points it lists (`SystemIntegration.test.tsx`, `SystemE2E.test.tsx`) don't exist and its `npm run test:system` was never defined — all three instructions broken, so both were deleted (`docs/analysis/documentation-review.md` had already flagged the doc "⚠️ Outdated" and recommended archiving). **Audit gate — the one architectural fork, escalated rather than defaulted:** the AC's literal `pip-audit + npm audit --audit-level=high` step **fails on the current tree** (frontend 20 high / 2 critical, still 7 high + 1 critical with `--omit=dev`; backend 64 advisories across 11 packages), so it would have red-lined `main` on the first push and been switched off within a week. Chosen (user-approved over advisory-only and over clearing the backlog now): a **baseline-allowlisted blocking gate** — `scripts/audit_gate.py` diffs live `pip-audit --format=json` + `npm audit --json` against a committed `security-baseline.json`, failing **only on advisories not already recorded**, so the 91 known ones become an explicit ledger (each entry naming its package and fix version) while anything new is red immediately. `npm audit` resolves straight from the lockfile — no `npm ci`, so the job is cheap. **Real advisories eliminated, not deferred:** measured against a pristine `main` worktree with the same command, **64 → 49 advisories (−15)**, with `cryptography`, `ecdsa` (the Minerva timing CVE the issue named) and `pyasn1` leaving the tree entirely — all three entered **solely** via `python-jose`, which had zero importers. **`passlib` removed in the issue's mandated 3-step order**, which mattered: it was imported at `app/core/security.py:1` and six endpoint modules import that file, so it loaded on *every request path* and a bare dep-drop would have broken the build — deleted `hash_password`/`verify_password`/`pwd_context` + the import, deleted the 5 covering tests (their only callers), *then* dropped the dep. **Second install path caught during docs sync** and fixed in a follow-up commit: `scripts/deploy.sh:32` and `scripts/deploy-fixed.sh:91` deploy production via `uv pip install -r requirements.txt`, **not** from `uv.lock`, so re-locking alone would have left python-jose/passlib/ecdsa shipping to production; the same 9 packages were removed from `requirements.txt` (a set matching **exactly** what `uv sync` uninstalled, cross-validating it) and the two entries from `requirements.in`, with `six` kept (cloudinary/ebooklib/python-dateutil still need it) and `ecdsa` dropped from its provenance comment. Deliberately **not** a full `uv pip compile` recompile — `requirements.in` carries loose constraints (`FastAPI` unpinned), so recompiling would have bumped every unrelated pin away from what pyproject pins and CI tests; `uv pip install --dry-run` resolves clean with none reappearing. **Tests:** 29 TDD pins in `scripts/test_audit_gate.py` (run in the audit job itself, so the gate that guards the repo is itself guarded), covering both tools' real schemas, the severity floor, GHSA-from-URL extraction, string-vs-dict `via` handling, and per-resolution-path dedupe. Two were written to pin non-obvious failure modes found by self-review: `evaluate()` must take the **scanned ecosystems explicitly** rather than inferring them from findings (inferring would strand 53 dead entries on the happy day an ecosystem goes fully clean), and `_read()` **fails closed** when an audit report lacks its top-level key, since a killed tool leaves valid-JSON-but-empty output that would otherwise read as "no vulnerabilities". **Review:** cross-family **codex** — opencode hung with zero output (the documented failure mode) so the fallback was used, disclosed on the PR. **1 finding, adopted:** `parse_npm_audit` silently dropped any `via` advisory whose `url` was absent or non-GHSA — a **false green**. Not reproducible on the current tree (all 115 real `via` objects carry url/source/severity, verified before fixing) but right in principle for a gate whose job is failing closed; advisory identity now falls back GHSA → npm numeric `source` → `npm-unidentified-<pkg>`, and only a *recognised* severity below the floor is skippable. **Mutation-verified** with codex's exact scenario injected into the real audit output: exits 1 naming `npm-source-123456` where it previously exited 0, with real-tree output unchanged (91, PASS), confirming the change is purely defensive. codex separately **ran** the workflow's `|| rc=$?` and `rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}`-after-`tee` idioms to confirm they preserve exit codes under `bash -e`, and found no dangling references to the removed symbols. Also gitignores `__pycache__`/`*.pyc` tree-wide — the `backend/`-scoped rules missed `scripts/` once it gained Python (caught by `git add -A` staging two `.pyc` files, the #52 lesson). Backend **1164 passed / 11 skipped, 92% coverage**; frontend **128 suites / 2240 passed**; eslint 0 errors, `tsc` clean. **Demo:** `docs/demos/2026-07-27-issue-342-supply-chain-automation.md`. **Known limitations:** the **91 baselined advisories remain unfixed** — this PR makes them explicit and blocks new ones but does not clear them; that needs major bumps (pillow 11→12, starlette 0.47→1.3, next/postcss/sharp) and its own regression pass. The gate reads `uv.lock` while production deploys from `requirements.txt`; both are cleaned here but they are **separately maintained and have already drifted** (`requirements.in` never gained `sentry-sdk` from #334) — worth a follow-up to generate one from the other, deliberately not widened into this PR. `npm audit` needs network in CI; a registry outage surfaces as a non-0/1 exit and fails the job loudly rather than passing silently.

Four tests inflated the green count while validating nothing, and the headline rich-text capability had no test that could prove it works. **Deletions** (#200/#210 precedent): `MockSystemFlow.test.tsx` (mocked `bookClient` wholesale, rendered no component, then asserted the mocks returned their own configured values, printing "All core workflows validated"), `TestInfrastructureIntegration.test.tsx` (asserted `typeof describe === 'function'` — it tested Jest; rendered only inline throwaway components; **grep-verified no importer** of its exported `testUtils`/`renderWithSuspense`/`renderWithErrorBoundary`), `example.test.tsx` (scaffold), and `backend/tests/test_debug_chapter_questions.py` (**zero asserts**, print-only — collected by pytest and unable to fail; the same service call is covered with real asserts by `test_services/test_question_generation_service.py`, and the near-identical sibling `test_debug_questions.py` makes the same `generate_questions_for_chapter` call with 3 asserts). **Root-cause sweep** rather than only the cited files: scanned all of `backend/tests` for collected-but-assertless files — 3 hits, each triaged individually; `test_e2e_no_mocks.py` **kept** (no asserts of its own but delegates to `SystemE2ETest.run()`, whose `raise_for_status()` calls give it teeth — not tautological). **Coverage rose** (statements 88.93→89.00, functions 85.20→85.56, lines 90.16→90.24, branches unchanged), proving the deletions carried no product coverage; suite 131 suites/2249 → 128/2240, and the −9 reconciles exactly (−15 deleted, +6 added). **New E2E** `frontend/src/e2e/editor-formatting.spec.ts`: TipTap is globally mocked in jsdom (`jest.setup.ts:267`) because it cannot run there, so the unit layer only ever sees that *a command ran*, and the existing E2E only inserted plain draft text — **grep-verified that no spec on `main` asserted a single formatting mark**. The new spec drives real chromium + real TipTap + the real backend, clicks the live toolbar and asserts **twice** per format (the mark in the live DOM *and* the tag in the auto-saved backend HTML — formatting that renders but never persists is still broken): Bold, Heading 2, Bullet List, plus toggle-off. It lands in `src/e2e/` (the root `playwright.config.ts` `testDir`), which `tests.yml` runs as a required check, so it genuinely gates. **Call-count assertion dropped**: `RichTextEditor.test.tsx`'s `expect(mockRunFn).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(7)` was a magic number over a shared mock — **demonstrated vacuous**: restoring it and wiring the live Bold button to `toggleItalic` leaves all 4 tests green, including the one named "toggles formatting when toolbar buttons are clicked". Replaced with per-command `it.each` assertions (Bold→`toggleBold`, H1→`toggleHeading({level:1})`, …) that fail and name the exact button under the same mutation. **Bonus finding from the sweep** — `test_system_e2e_simplified.py` printed `Has TOC: False` and reported `PASSED`: **two defects cancelling out**, since `verify_system()` read `book["table_of_contents"]` (the *Mongo document's* field name, which `BookResponse` does not declare, so `.get()` returned `{}` unconditionally and the check could never be true **in any state**) and `run()` discarded the result. Now reads `GET /books/{id}/toc` and asserts it. **Every new assertion mutation-verified**: Bold→italic turns both Bold E2E tests RED (heading/list unaffected) and exactly 1 of 7 jest tests RED; skipping TOC generation fails the backend assert by name. A first attempt added a 17-line `accept_toc()` step on the assumption that `POST /generate-toc` only returns a candidate — **the mutation check disproved it** (removing the step still passed; generate-toc persists on its own), so the step and its confidently-wrong docstring were deleted, leaving a 2-line fix. **Review:** cross-family **opencode/GLM-5.2 — no Critical, no Major**; it independently re-verified the four load-bearing assumptions (toolbar `title=` attributes, AI mock method-name match, `/toc` response shape, `BookResponse` lacking `table_of_contents`) and specifically cleared the save-race question (the typing-triggered save writes `<p>text</p>`, which is not a substring of any polled fragment, so an intermediate un-formatted save cannot satisfy the poll). 1 of 5 Minor findings adopted: the standalone `'<ul>'` poll was weak (any list anywhere satisfied it), folded into the text-specific `<ul><li><p>text</p></li></ul>` and **re-run green rather than assumed**. **Demo:** `docs/demos/2026-07-27-issue-341-hollow-tests-rich-text-e2e.md`. Frontend **128 suites / 2240 passed**, backend **1198 passed / 11 skipped**, new E2E **4/4**, `tsc` clean, eslint 0 errors. **Follow-up #375**: `EditorToolbar.tsx` has zero production importers — its `aria-label` a11y suite (#50, WCAG 2.1 AA) passes against a component no user renders, while the live inline toolbar in `ChapterEditor.tsx` has `title=` and **no `aria-label`**; same hollow-test class plus a real a11y gap, scoped out rather than doubling this PR. **Known limitations:** the E2E's exact-substring fragments couple to TipTap's serialization (a StarterKit bump could break them — they fail loudly, and loosening them would weaken the one thing the spec exists to prove), and the spec relies on Playwright's accessible-name fallback to `title` (empirically confirmed, and #375 would replace it with real `aria-label`s). One transient `VoiceTextInput.test.tsx` `waitFor` timing failure appeared in a single `--coverage` run under concurrent-server load and did not reproduce across three subsequent full runs or in isolation — pre-existing, in a file this PR does not touch.

All 10 AI endpoints declare `Depends(get_ai_usage_quota())` (cost control, #173) and `Depends(get_entitlement_checker(...))` (paywall hook, #174), but the suite globally no-ops the quota and the entitlement gate self-disables under `BYPASS_AUTH`, so **either dependency could be deleted from 9 of the 10 endpoints with the whole suite staying green** — verified, not assumed: with `Depends(get_entitlement_checker("enhance_text"))` deleted from `enhance-text`, the pre-existing `test_entitlement_gate.py` + `test_ai_usage_quota.py` + `test_rate_limit_routes.py` still reported 22 passed. Entitlement had route-level coverage on exactly 1 of 10 endpoints; quota had none at HTTP level. **Fix:** `tests/test_api/test_ai_gate_routes.py` — a completeness pin that walks the live FastAPI route tree and names any endpoint that lost either dep (plus a drift guard for new AI routes), restricted-plan→402 and entitled-plan→not-402 parameterized across all 10, and real-quota-at-cap→429 (real Mongo counting, production headers asserted) parameterized across all 10. **Enabler:** `conftest.fake_get_ai_usage_quota` returned a *fresh* `_always_allow` closure per route, so there was no single `dependency_overrides` key to re-arm the real quota — that is *why* no HTTP-level quota test existed. It now returns ONE shared module-level `noop_ai_quota` (mirroring `noop_rate_limiter`), and a new `arm_real_ai_quota` fixture swaps in the genuine quota on every AI route at once, mirroring `arm_real_rate_limiter` from #199. The `_walk_effective_routes` / `_route_dependency_calls` helpers moved out of `test_rate_limit_routes.py` into `tests/route_introspection.py` so both pins share one walker (imported as `tests.route_introspection`, the same shape as the existing `tests.db_guard` — deliberately *not* `tests.conftest`, which double-executes conftest and rebinds the shared no-ops). Mutation-verified in both directions: deleting the quota dep from `generate-draft` fails the quota pin **and** that route's 429 test; deleting the entitlement dep from `enhance-text` fails the entitlement pin **and** that route's 402 test — each naming the exact route.
- **Session cookie `sameSite` `"none"` → `"lax"`, restoring CSRF protection (#339, P1.7, security)**: frontend + backend. The better-auth session cookie was issued `sameSite:"none"` while the backend authenticates **from `request.cookies`** (`app/core/better_auth_session.py:48`) with `allow_credentials=True` and no CSRF token or Origin check — so any site could form-POST the victim's cookie at a state-changing endpoint. Multipart uploads (`POST /users/me/avatar`, book cover) are CORS-**simple** requests that trigger no preflight, so CORS blocked *reading* the response but never the write. **Fix:** `sameSite:"lax"`. The `"none"` was never necessary — its code comment conflated same-**site** with same-**origin**: same-site is computed on the registrable domain and ignores port and subdomain, so `localhost:3000 → localhost:8000` and `dev.autoauthor.app → api.dev.autoauthor.app` (both `autoauthor.app`) are same-site and `lax` transmits normally. **AC2 (keep `none` + a double-submit token or Origin allowlist) is N/A — verified, not assumed:** better-auth runs email+password + `twoFactor()` only (no social/OAuth/magic-link POST-back), and although Stripe **is** wired up, `success_url`/`cancel_url`/`return_url` are reached by a **top-level GET navigation** (which `lax` sends) and `POST /webhooks/stripe` is deliberately unauthenticated, verified by HMAC over the raw body rather than a cookie. Cookie attributes moved to a new `frontend/src/lib/auth-cookies.ts` purely so they are unit-testable — `auth.ts` imports `server-only` + the MongoDB driver and cannot load under jsdom; `getCookieDomain` moved verbatim, `secure`/`httpOnly`/`domain` unchanged. **AC3:** `validate_production_security()` (`app/main.py`) now rejects a wildcard in `BACKEND_CORS_ORIGINS`, verified against the installed Starlette (0.47.2) source — `if self.allow_all_origins and has_cookie:` mirrors the *requested* origin back, so `"*"` + cookie auth hands any site a readable credentialed response. Deliberately **fatal in every environment, not just production** (tightened after cross-family review): staging is internet-reachable, and no committed config sets a wildcard (`deploy-staging.yml`, `scripts/deploy*.sh`, `backend/.env.example` all list explicit origins), so this blocks a misconfiguration rather than a working setup. **Tests:** 8 frontend pins on the cookie attributes + domain derivation, 5 backend pins on the CORS guard (wildcard alone, wildcard among real origins, comma-string form, non-production, explicit-origins-pass). **Mutation-verified:** restoring `sameSite:"none"` turns the cookie pin RED; disabling the guard turns 4 of 5 CORS pins RED (the 5th is a documented regression guard, correctly green on both sides). 100% diff coverage on the new backend lines. **Review:** cross-family **opencode/GLM — "No discrete defect is introduced by this change"**, no Critical/Major; it independently confirmed the Starlette behavior, the absence of a cross-site flow, and that the tests are not vacuous. Two of its four Minor findings were adopted (the guard no longer only warns outside production; the `allow_origins` test fixture no longer lists the API's own origin) and its comment-precision nit applied — Starlette's trigger is `allow_all_origins AND has_cookie`, so flipping `allow_credentials` off does **not** disable the reflection. **Demo** (`docs/demos/2026-07-27-issue-339-samesite-lax-csrf.md`): the attack was reproduced in real Chromium on both sides against the same Mongo — an attacker page on `127.0.0.1` (cross-site from `localhost`) auto-submits a multipart POST; on **main the session cookie rides along (CSRF works)**, on the **branch the request still arrives but carries no cookie (CSRF blocked)**, while the legitimate same-site app→API credentialed fetch carries the cookie on **both** sides, proving `lax` broke nothing. AC3 shown as main-boots-and-reflects-`https://evil.example` vs branch-refuses-to-start. Backend **1165 passed / 11 skipped, 92% coverage**; frontend **2248 passed** (one unrelated `ChapterEditor.saveStatus` 5s timeout under load from two concurrent dev servers — re-run in isolation 14/14 green). **Known limitations:** `lax` does not defend against a *same-site* attacker (XSS on a sibling subdomain of `autoauthor.app`). The review also flagged, as **pre-existing and out of scope**, that `GET /{book_id}/chapters/{chapter_id}/content` writes `chapter_access_logs` — a state-changing GET, which `lax` does not cover for top-level navigations.
  - **Status**: ✅ Complete

### 2026-07-25
- **Question index creation no longer skips every index after a failure (#338, P1.6, data-integrity)**: backend-only. `ensure_question_indexes` (`app/db/questions.py`) wrapped all six `create_index` calls in a **single** try/except that only logged. When the unique `question_responses.question_user_idx` build failed — e.g. the collection already held duplicate responses — the exception aborted the whole block and every later index was silently skipped, including the `question_ratings` uniqueness guard. The app then served writes without the guarantee `save_question_response`'s non-atomic find-then-insert relies on (#242), behind a generic log line that didn't name what was missing. **Fix:** index definitions moved to a `_QUESTION_INDEXES` table `(collection, keys, name, unique)`; each is created in its own try/except, matching the `ensure_user_indexes` pattern in `app/db/user.py`. Errors name the specific collection + index, and unique failures state plainly that uniqueness is unenforced until an operator dedupes. Still never raises — index creation must not block startup. All six indexes keep identical names, keys and `unique` flags. **Tests:** 1 new, inducing a genuine `DuplicateKeyError` from real Mongo (pre-seeded duplicate responses, no mocking) and asserting the indexes *after* the failing one are still built; verified red-before (both later indexes absent, collections left with only `_id_`) / green-after. The test also asserts its own ordering precondition against `_QUESTION_INDEXES`, so reordering the table fails loudly instead of silently making the test a no-op. Verified against real Mongo that passing an explicit `unique=False` does not raise `IndexOptionsConflict` against indexes built by the old code, which omitted the kwarg — existing deployments rebuild cleanly. **Known limitation:** a failed unique index is logged at ERROR but startup proceeds, so #242's protection can be silently absent in production until an operator dedupes and restarts; wiring that ERROR to a Sentry alert would close the loop and was left out of scope. A fully unreachable database now logs six errors instead of one.

### 2026-07-24
- **TOC add/update/delete/reorder now write under an optimistic lock (#337, P1.5, data-integrity)**: backend-only. `_add_chapter_internal`, `_update_chapter_internal`, `_delete_chapter_internal`, and `_reorder_chapters_internal` (`app/db/toc_transactions.py`) each read the whole `table_of_contents`, mutated it in memory, and `$set` it back filtered **only on `{_id}`** — no `owner_id`, no version guard, no `modified_count` check. On standalone (non-replica-set) Mongo `session` is `None`, so this was plain last-write-wins: a concurrent autosave (`apply_chapter_content_update`) and a chapter mutation silently clobbered each other. **Fix:** extracted a single compare-and-swap helper `_set_toc_guarded` — `update_one` filtered on `{_id, owner_id, table_of_contents.version}` with an `$exists:false` fallback for legacy version-less TOCs, plus a `modified_count == 0` check — and routed all four through it, then **deduped** the pre-existing `update_chapter_statuses_with_version_guard` (#159) onto the same helper so there is one implementation of the guard. A companion `_version_guard()` snapshots the read-time version **before** the callers mutate the TOC dict in place; reading it after mutation would guard on the value about to be written and never detect a conflict. `modified_count` (not `matched_count`) is sufficient *only* because every caller increments `version`, so a matched-but-unmodified write is impossible — documented in the helper as an invariant future writes must preserve. On a filter miss the helper **re-reads to distinguish** a genuine conflict from a book deleted or reassigned mid-operation, so the API answers 404/403 rather than a misleading "modified by another user" 409. Endpoint mapping: `Version conflict` → **409** added to create/update/delete chapter in `app/api/endpoints/chapters.py` (previously these fell through to a 400 carrying the raw internal message). **Tests:** 16 new — interleaved-writer tests parametrized across all four ops that fire a competing TOC write in the read→write window and assert both that the loser raises and that the winner's data survives byte-intact, plus legacy version-less, owner-reassigned, and book-deleted variants, and three API-level tests asserting 409. Verified red-before/green-after (8 lost-update assertions failed pre-fix; the 409 tests returned 400 with the endpoint mapping stashed). `toc_transactions.py` coverage 94%. **Known limitation:** on a *replica set* these four still run inside `session.start_transaction()`, where a concurrent commit surfaces as a WriteConflict → 500 rather than the 409 the standalone path now returns — pre-existing behavior the issue explicitly acknowledged ("Prod Atlas mitigates via transaction abort"), left unchanged because it cannot be verified without a replica-set test fixture. Follow-up option: drop the transaction wrapper for these single-document writes, as `update_chapter_statuses_with_version_guard` already does deliberately.
- **Chapter-question `status` filter resolved before pagination (#336, P1.4, bug/correctness)**: backend-only, PR #367. `get_questions_for_chapter` (`app/db/questions.py`) paged the `questions` collection with `status` **absent from the Mongo query**, then dropped non-matching rows from the already-sliced page in Python — a workaround for the fact that response status lives in a *different* collection (`question_responses`) and so couldn't be expressed in the initial query. Because `skip`/`limit` walked **raw** documents, every count in the response described a different set: `total` was the post-filter length of **one page**, the internal `count_documents` ignored `status`, and `pages`/`has_more` derived from that unfiltered count. **Fix:** resolve the status-matching `_id` set **first** (one projection query for the chapter's ids + one batched responses lookup, both already user/book/chapter-scoped by the same `query`) and fold it in as `_id: {"$in": [...]}`, so `sort`/`skip`/`limit`/`count` all operate on the filtered set; return the real `count_documents(query)` as `total`; delete the post-pagination filter. Took the AC's **first-listed id-set branch over `$lookup`** deliberately: `question_responses.question_id` is a *string* of `questions._id` (ObjectId), so an aggregation needs `$toString` (Mongo 4.0+) plus `$facet` for count-and-page — the id-set form is smaller and version-agnostic, and the two extra reads run **only** when `status` is passed (unfiltered path untouched). Page enrichment now reuses the map the filter already built instead of re-querying, preserving the #214 batched-lookup (no N+1) property; `has_response`/`response_status` semantics are byte-equivalent (the old `if response:` was always key-presence, since a Mongo doc is never an empty dict, and the `r.get("status", "draft")` default is kept verbatim rather than "improved" to `or "draft"`, which would differ on a stored empty-string status). **Demo-corrected impact claim:** the issue says a client "can permanently miss matching questions"; verified precisely — a client that *blindly follows the inflated `pages`* does sweep everything by accident (paging the unfiltered set visits every document). The real losses are that `total` reports **1** when four questions match, pages come back half-empty, and a page can be **entirely empty while matches exist** (`?status=draft&limit=2` returned `{"questions": [], "total": 0, "pages": 4, "has_more": true}` — three fields describing three different sets — on a chapter that *has* a draft), which reads as "no results" and is unrecoverable. **Tests:** 9 real-Mongo pins in `tests/test_db/test_question_status_pagination.py` (multi-page completeness + disjointness, no-empty-page, per-status, user scoping, composition with `category`, unfiltered-`total` regression, unknown-status characterization) + an endpoint wire-contract test. **Mutation-verified three ways:** 5 of the 9 go RED against the **real pre-fix code** in a pristine `main` worktree (not a synthetic mutation); disabling only the id-set resolution fails the 5 status pins while the unfiltered-`total` pin still passes; reverting only `total=total` fails the 3 total-accuracy pins. The 4 pins green on both sides are documented in-file as regression guards, not bug demonstrations. **Reviews:** cross-family **codex clean pre-PR and post-PR** ("did not identify a discrete introduced bug"); **GLM CI review "✅ no defects found"**, independently verifying the ObjectId round-trip can't raise, the `$in: []` empty-match case is coherent, status semantics match the old filter exactly, filters compose rather than replace, and all three `total` consumers stay compatible. opencode was occupied by a foreign-cwd session then hung 12+ min (the documented condition) → codex fallback, disclosed on the PR. **Demo** (`docs/demos/2026-07-24-issue-336-question-status-pagination.md`): two real uvicorn servers (pristine `main` :8801 vs branch :8802) against the same Mongo, so code is the only variable; `showboat verify` reproduces with diffs on pytest wall-clock timings only. Backend suite **1105 passed / 11 skipped, 92% coverage**; `questions.py` ruff violations drop 14 → 11 (the deleted filter held the long lines). CI Backend + Frontend + E2E (Playwright) + GLM review + GitGuardian + Snyk all green. **Known limitations:** `total` changes meaning from page-length to filtered-set size (what the AC requires; no production consumer reads it — `bookClient.ts` paginates on `pages`, and `question_generation_service.py:206` compares `total >= expected`, which only gets more lenient). An unrecognised `status` still returns unfiltered results — pre-existing, never validated, pinned by a characterization test; tightening it to 422 is a separate breaking change. The bug is **latent in the shipped UI**: no production frontend passes `status` (`QuestionContainer.tsx:83` calls with no options), which is also why that call site only ever loads the first 10 questions of a chapter — noted, out of scope.
  - **Status**: ✅ Complete

- **`/health` verifies Mongo + required secrets so broken releases fail the deploy gate (#333, P1.1, reliability/observability)**: backend-only, PR #357. `/health` returned a static `{"status":"healthy"}` with **zero dependency checks**, so the deploy gate (`curl -f .../api/v1/health`, `deploy-staging.yml:364`) only proved uvicorn accepts a socket — a release with a wrong `MONGODB_URI`, un-allowlisted Atlas IP, or a missing `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` **passed the health check and was promoted** while every real request 500s. **Fix (endpoint only):** the probe now awaits `asyncio.wait_for(get_database().command("ping"), timeout=5s)` (catches wrong URI / un-allowlisted Atlas IP / Mongo down — bounded so a broken/slow Mongo fails *fast* instead of hanging on the app client's 30s `serverSelectionTimeoutMS`) and asserts required secrets are configured, returning **503 + the failing component** in a `checks` body on any failure. The CI/test placeholder secrets are rejected **only in production** (via the existing `is_production_env()`), so a release that never wired in a real key fails the gate while CI — which uses the placeholders in a non-production env — stays green. **No deploy-workflow change needed (AC3):** the gate already runs `curl -f` in a retry-then-`exit 1` loop, so a persistent 503 now fails a broken release — the gate was always correct, only the static-200 probe lied. **The event-loop subtlety:** the import-time motor client binds to the first sync-`TestClient` request's portal loop, so a later test's `TestClient` (different loop) makes a real `.command("ping")` raise `RuntimeError`; the logic tests therefore **mock `get_database`** (deterministic) and a single integration test drives the endpoint through the async client factory (motor rebound in one loop) to prove the **real** ping call is correct. **Tests (mutation-verified):** healthy→200, Mongo-down→503 naming mongodb, missing-secret→503 naming the key, production-placeholder→503, placeholder-allowed-outside-prod, fast-fail on slow Mongo, real-Mongo ping→200. Mutation 1 (force always-200) turns the three 503 tests RED; mutation 2 (drop the production-placeholder teeth) turns the production test RED. Backward compatible — existing consumers read `data["status"]`, still present. **Reviews:** cross-family **codex "clean"** pre-PR; **GLM CI review "✅ no defects found"** (independently verified against the repo, incl. that `deploy-staging.yml` writes **both** `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `OPENAI_AUTOAUTHOR_API_KEY` from one secret ⇒ a missing secret empties both ⇒ the presence check fires on the **real staging gate**, not just theoretically; no secret values leaked — only `type(e).__name__` + key *names*; `curl -f` treats 503 as failure). Demo (`docs/demos/2026-07-23-issue-333-health-check-deps.md`): identical production env, **main returns static 200 (`curl -f` exit 0, broken release promoted) vs branch 503 naming the missing key (`curl -f` exit 22, release blocked)** — same config, opposite outcome, with `mongodb: ok` proving the real ping ran. **Known limitations:** secret checks are presence, not liveness — an *expired* OpenAI key or a wrong-but-nonempty `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` still passes (verifying those needs a live API call / a token to verify against, neither appropriate for a probe; the AC scopes this to keys being **present**). Mongo gets a real liveness ping. `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`'s presence check is mostly documentation — the config startup validator already blocks an empty/CI-placeholder secret in production. A totally-unreachable Mongo also kills startup (index creation grinds through 30s timeouts) so the release fails either way; the probe covers the boots-green-but-misconfigured case.
  - **Status**: ✅ Complete
- **Password reset fails loudly when the email provider is unconfigured — no more fake success (#332, P0.3, bug/security)**: frontend + deploy-config, PR #356. Sign-in advertised password reset, but in production the reset **sent no email** while showing "Check your email" — account recovery silently broken for a SaaS. Root cause in `frontend/src/lib/email.ts::sendPasswordResetEmail`: when `EMAIL_SERVICE_PROVIDER` was unset, it `console.warn`'d and **returned silently** in every non-production environment (and threw only a generic message in production), while `auth.ts`'s deliberate anti-enumeration `void (async…)()` swallowed the throw — so the failure surfaced nowhere. The three `EMAIL_*` vars also appeared in neither `.env.example` nor the deploy workflow, so an operator had no signal they were required. **Fix (root cause = one decision branch):** an unset/unknown provider now emits a loud, greppable `[email] Password reset email NOT sent — …` `console.error` naming the missing vars **AND throws, in EVERY environment** — the silent warn-and-return branch that enabled the fake success is deleted. The dev-console convenience path (`NODE_ENV=development` + no provider → console-dump, no throw) is unchanged. **Anti-enumeration preserved:** the forgot-password UI still shows generic success; `auth.ts`'s `void` IIFE swallows the throw by design, so observability lives in **server logs**, never the HTTP response (GLM CI review independently confirmed this invariant holds — the sole runtime caller is `auth.ts:144-161`). AC1: `.env.example` documents `EMAIL_SERVICE_PROVIDER`/`EMAIL_SERVICE_API_KEY`/`EMAIL_FROM_ADDRESS` (+ optional `EMAIL_FROM_NAME`), flagged REQUIRED in staging/prod; `deploy-staging.yml` writes the three secrets into the frontend `.env.production` (loaded by `next start` at runtime). **Tests:** first tests for `email.ts` (7 pins — unconfigured/unknown provider loud+throw, resend/sendgrid send paths, resend-no-key, dev-console convenience); the two observability pins are **mutation-verified RED** against the restored old silent behavior. **Reviews:** cross-family **codex** substance-clean ("runtime behavior appears aligned with the stated invariants"); its lone P1 (server-only breaks the test) was a **false positive** — codex's own jest run failed on `EROFS: read-only /tmp` and never saw the result, while `next/jest` disables `server-only` and the suite passes 7/7 (opencode/GLM hung on the pre-PR attempt, the documented flake). **GLM CI review "✅ no defects found."** Demo (`docs/demos/2026-07-23-issue-332-…md`): main-vs-branch behavior differential (old silent path = 2 RED observability pins = the exact fake-success bug; branch = loud error + throw). CI Backend + Frontend + E2E (Playwright) + GLM review + GitGuardian + Snyk all green. **Known limitations:** AC2 (verify a **real** delivery on staging) is a manual ops step — the actual `EMAIL_*` secret values must be set in GitHub and staging redeployed before a real email sends; this PR makes the previously-silent misconfiguration loud so that step is verifiable. The disabled Clerk-era `deploy-production.yml.disabled` is not retrofitted (out of scope; staging is the live deploy path).
  - **Status**: ✅ Complete
- **Continuous voice dictation appends every segment — stale-closure data loss fixed (#330, P0.1, bug/data-loss)**: frontend-only, PR #354. `VoiceTextInput.initializeSpeechRecognition` built the `onresult` handler once at recording start, capturing the `value` prop in its closure. With `continuous = true`, every finalized segment computed `onChange(value + segment + ' ')` against the **same captured `value`**, so each new segment **overwrote** the previous one — dictating a multi-sentence interview answer kept only the last sentence (silent loss of user-authored content in an advertised feature). The test/mock fired exactly one final result, so the accumulation path was never exercised and the bug shipped green. **Fix (canonical React stale-closure):** a `valueRef` mirrored to the latest prop via an effect; the long-lived handler reads `valueRef.current` (always fresh) instead of the frozen closure var, so successive segments append. Removed the now-unused `value` from the `useCallback` deps (a false dep — recognition isn't rebuilt mid-recording). Adapts the summary page's `setSummary(prev => …)` accumulation intent to a `(value: string) => void` callback prop that has no functional-updater form; both `onresult` write paths fixed (cursor-insert branch is dead in voice mode since the Textarea unmounts → `textareaRef.current` null → the append branch is what dictation actually hits). **Tests (mutation-verified RED):** extended `speechRecognition.ts` mock with a `sequence` config (interim + multiple finals on successive timers) + a deterministic `emitResult`; new `Continuous dictation (accumulation)` suite fires **two separate final results** through a controlled wrapper (state fed back via `onChange`, the `QuestionDisplay` contract) and asserts **BOTH survive**, plus an interim+two-finals sequence test — reverting `valueRef.current` → captured `value` turns both RED (only the last segment survives). **Reviews:** cross-family **codex "no introduced bugs"** + **GLM CI review "no defects found"** (independently verified the ref idiom, both write paths, the deps removal, the consumer contract, the dead cursor-insert branch, and the harmless mock error-path `onend` change). Demo: bug-vs-fix outcome differential on the exact acceptance scenario (real-voice E2E infeasible — headless `SpeechRecognition` unavailable, #56/#57 precedent). Local: VoiceTextInput 8/8, integration 5/5, typecheck clean, eslint 0 errors. **Coverage note:** the mock lives in the `coveragePathIgnorePatterns`-excluded `src/__tests__/mocks/` path (#328) and the fix adds **zero new product-code functions** (a ref + effect + variable renames), so the functions-coverage gate is untouched; CI Backend + Frontend + GLM review + Snyk all green. **Known limitations:** none.
  - **Status**: ✅ Complete
- **Frontend global functions coverage gate goes honestly green — end the every-PR `--admin` override (#328, frontend/CI)**: frontend-only (jest config + tests). Filed from the #295/PR #327 merge. The CI `Check coverage threshold` step enforces `global.functions >= 85%`, but `main` itself had sat at **~84.56–84.64%** since ~#215 (2026-07-20), so `Frontend Tests` was red on essentially every frontend PR even with 100% tests passing — forcing an `--admin` branch-protection override on #215/#237/#239/#276/#280/#327. **Root cause = wrong denominator, not under-tested product code**: the gap was **~4 covered functions** (954/1127), and two files in the denominator (`src/__tests__/fixtures/chapterTabsFixtures.ts`, `src/__tests__/mocks/speechRecognition.ts`) are **test infrastructure, not shippable code**, only counted because jest was never told to exclude them. Took **option 1 (raise coverage honestly, keep the 85 bar)** over lowering the threshold or per-dir configs — a 4-function gap doesn't justify weakening the gate. Two changes: (1) `jest.config.cjs` gains `coveragePathIgnorePatterns` excluding `src/__tests__/fixtures/`, `src/__tests__/mocks/`, `src/__mocks__/` — a **surgical denominator fix** (deliberately NOT `collectCoverageFrom`, which would force-collect never-imported files at 0% and make coverage *worse*); applies to both local and CI since both run jest. → functions 84.64% → 85.04%. (2) Added **genuine unit tests of real logic** (not UI-render theater) for currently-untested pure functions: `exportHelpers.ts` (`formatFileSize` log/pow math + 0-byte branch, `estimateExportTime` floor + pdf-vs-other rate, `downloadBlob` DOM flow via jsdom mocks) and `bookClient.ts` (`enhanceChapterText`/`enhanceVoiceTranscription`/`getExportTemplates` contract + error branches). Final: **functions 85.58% (944/1103)**, ~6.5-function headroom so one new component won't re-trip it; statements 89.34 / branches 79.72 / lines 90.49 — all four gates clear. The now-working gate restores the #118 per-PR enforcement loop (a real drop below 85% fails and prompts tests). **Fallback if it recurs (documented):** pin an honest no-regression floor + ratchet. **Known limitations:** frontend function coverage naturally erodes as components accrue untested handlers; headroom is finite but the gate now catches regressions instead of blocking everything.
  - **Status**: ✅ Complete

- **`PUT /question-responses` stores an honest completion status; dead `toc-readiness` composite deleted (#276, P3.13, backend)**: backend-only, PR #325. Follow-up filed from the #203 review (codex flagged it twice, verified out of scope there). Two defects in the pre-TOC endpoints of `books.py`: (1) the PUT endpoint **unconditionally stamped `status:"completed"`** + returned `ready_for_toc_generation:true` for any list — a trap now that #203 wires the TOC wizard's debounced auto-save to it, so partial sets are routinely persisted mid-typing; (2) `GET /toc-readiness` computed a composite `is_ready_for_toc` + `next_steps` from questions/responses that **both return branches discarded** (they return only summary-analysis fields) — computed-then-discarded dead code. **Verified harmless today** (no live consumer reads `status`, `ready_for_toc_generation`, the composite, or `next_steps` — swept frontend + backend), but a latent trap. Took the AC's **derive-server-side** branch: a set is `completed` only when **every** clarifying question is covered by a response — matched **by question text (coverage), not a count**, so duplicate/stale responses can't reach the count while a current question stays unanswered; fails toward `draft` when there are no questions to compare against (unknowable completeness → never a false `completed`). `ready_for_toc_generation` mirrors it; `clarifying_questions.status` (`answered`/`pending`) tracks it too. - **`ProtectedRoute` client guard preserves the `?redirect` deep-link on session expiry (#239, P3.12, bug/ux)**: frontend-only, PR #324. The client-side `ProtectedRoute` guard fires on a session expiry with **no navigation** (a background auth check fails — the `middleware.ts` `?redirect` layer only runs on full navigations), and it pushed a **bare** `/auth/sign-in`, so the user lost their return path and always re-landed on `/dashboard`. Fix mirrors the middleware exactly: read `usePathname()` and push `` `/auth/sign-in?redirect=${encodeURIComponent(pathname)}` `` — the sign-in page already re-validates the param via `sanitizeRedirectPath` (#184, same-origin-only, decode-checked, `/dashboard` fallback), so no new trust surface. **Blast radius beyond the one line:** `usePathname()` is a hook called *unconditionally*, so every test rendering the guard (even auth-bypassed ones) started throwing `usePathname is not a function` — swept all four sibling suites that render it (`AuthPersistence`, `ProfilePage`, `ProfilePageDelete`, `ProfilePageSave`) to mock it + assert the deep-link contract. Full frontend suite **125 suites, 2209 passed / 5 skipped**; Backend Tests + GitGuardian + Snyk pass. **CI note:** merged via admin override past the **pre-existing** global functions-coverage gate (84.56% vs 85%) — identical to `main` (#215/#237 shipped through it 2026-07-20/21); this PR adds **zero new function definitions** (only a hook call + a dep-array entry in the already-covered component), so it landed on the exact baseline and did not regress it. **Known limitations:** `usePathname()` is path-only, so a return path drops its query string (e.g. `?tab=export`) — but this matches the existing middleware behavior (also `pathname`-only), keeping the two layers consistent; the related 2FA-path redirect drop is #237 (already shipped).

### 2026-07-21 (latest)
- **2FA sign-in preserves the `?redirect` deep-link (#237, P3.11, bug/ux)**: frontend-only, PR #323. Follow-up filed from the #236 open-redirect review (pre-existing behavior). A 2FA-enabled sign-in with `?redirect=<path>` returned early on `twoFactorRedirect` (handing off to the `twoFactorClient` navigation), and `verify-2fa/page.tsx` hardcoded `router.push('/dashboard')` — so the deep-link target was dropped and the user always landed on `/dashboard`. Fix threads the target across the 2FA hop via a one-shot `sessionStorage` key `auth:postVerifyRedirect`: the sign-in page **always** writes the already-sanitized `redirect` (defaulting to `/dashboard`) before the 2FA nav, and the verify-2fa page reads+clears it after successful verification, re-running it through `sanitizeRedirectPath` at the point of use (defense in depth, as #237 required), falling back to `/dashboard`; the stash survives a *failed* attempt so a retry still honors it. **Cross-family codex review** (opencode occupied by a concurrent session — the documented [[opencode-concurrent-hang]] condition) caught a real **Major** in the initial branch commit: the 2FA branch originally *skipped* writing on the `/dashboard` target, so a stale stash from an abandoned earlier 2FA attempt in the same tab was consumable by a later no-redirect sign-in (wrong destination) — fixed by always writing the current sanitized redirect (default `/dashboard` overwrites/clears stale), mutation-verified RED against the skip-guard. Tests: `SignInPage.test.tsx` (stash-on-2FA + stale-stash overwrite) + new `verify-2fa/__tests__/page.test.tsx` (honors+clears stash, falls back, re-sanitizes tampered value, keeps stash on failed attempt) — **15/15 pass**, both behavior pins mutation-verified RED. Demo: main-vs-branch outcome differential using the **real** `SignInForm`/`VerifyTwoFactorPage` components — the branch's suites run against main's page code go RED on `redirects to the stashed deep-link` (main hardcodes `/dashboard` — the exact bug), GREEN on the branch. **Known limitations:** none — frontend-only, self-contained; reuses existing `sanitizeRedirectPath` (#184). **CI note:** merged via admin override past a **pre-existing** global functions-coverage gate (84.56% vs 85%) that is red on `main` identically (#215 shipped through it 2026-07-20); #237 did not regress it (verify-2fa page now fully covered, +6 passing tests) — the coverage baseline is a separate repo-wide issue.

### 2026-07-20
- **`create_chapter` returns 400 for a bad `parent_id` instead of a masked 404 (#158, P3.9, bug/backend)**: backend-only, PR #321. Follow-up filed from #157's CodeRabbit triage. In `create_chapter` (`app/api/endpoints/chapters.py`), the `except ValueError` chain tested the generic `"not found" in str(e).lower()` **before** the specific `elif "Parent chapter not found"` — and `"Parent chapter not found"` *contains* the substring `"not found"`, so the generic branch always fired first and the intended 400 branch was **unreachable dead code**. A `POST /books/{id}/chapters` with `level>1` and an unknown `parent_id` therefore returned **404 "Book not found"** instead of **400 "Parent chapter not found"**. Root cause = order-of-evaluation shadowing (broad substring check before the narrow one). Fix: reorder the specific `"Parent chapter not found"` (400) check first, then generic `"not found"` (404), `"not authorized"` (403), fallback — and add `raise ... from e` chaining to all four branches (B904). The 404/403/fallback branches are **behavior-unchanged for their own cases**; only the previously-shadowed parent branch flips 404→400. **Plan drift (verified):** CodeRabbit's plan (2026-07-01) said to edit `books.py` because `chapters.py` "does not exist" — that plan predates #157 (merged 2026-07-02) which extracted the chapter endpoints into `chapters.py`; the issue body already pointed to `chapters.py` (ground truth) so the fix targets it, not the stale plan. Tests: flipped the pinned `test_create_chapter_bad_parent_returns_404` → `_returns_400` (asserts `400` + `"Parent chapter not found"`) + updated the module-docstring behavioral note; **mutation-verified RED** (404 against pre-fix code) → **GREEN** (76 passed in `test_books_chapters_crud_coverage.py` + `test_toc_transactions.py`). The transaction-layer `test_add_subchapter_parent_not_found` is untouched — it asserts the raw `ValueError`, unaffected by the HTTP mapping. Review: cross-family **`codex` pre-PR "clean to merge"** (confirmed no branch newly shadowed/unreachable, 404/403/fallback unchanged for their cases, no case where "Parent chapter not found" should legitimately be 404); GLM CI review skipped (18-line diff below the size threshold). Demo: same acceptance request → **404 "Book not found" on main vs 400 "Parent chapter not found" on branch**, both observed against the real mounted app. CI **Backend Tests pass (2m45s), Frontend Tests pass (3m51s)**, GitGuardian + Snyk pass. **Known limitations:** two pre-existing `ruff F841` warnings (unused `updated_chapter`/`success` in `update_chapter`/`delete_chapter`) predate this PR — verbatim from the #94/#157 extraction, outside this diff's scope, left untouched (same precedent as #214).
  - **Status**: ✅ Complete

- **Guard test-DB teardown against non-test/remote URIs — no more Atlas-wipe risk (#211, P3.5, test-trust/data-loss)**: backend-tests-only, PR #317. `conftest.py` drops its database (`drop_database` in `motor_reinit_db`; `_sync_users/_books/_logs.drop()` in both client factories) with the target derived **purely from `TEST_MONGO_URI`** and no assertion it's local/`*test*`. The project's real Mongo is Atlas and the repo has a history of stray env vars, so an accidental Atlas URI would silently wipe real `users`/`books`/`audit_logs`. **Line drift (verified):** the issue cited `conftest.py:104,131,289-291,412-414` but the file had been edited since — the actual drop sites today are `167,193` (motor_reinit_db) + `353-355,477-478` (factory teardowns). **Fix = single import-time choke point** (root cause, not per-call-site): every drop runs at fixture-execution time, always *after* conftest finishes importing, so one guard at import makes it physically impossible for any of the 6 drop sites (or a 7th added later) to run against an unsafe URI. New pure `backend/tests/db_guard.py::assert_safe_test_db(uri, db_name)` called once in conftest right after `_TEST_DB_NAME` resolves and **before the sync client connects** — allow if the DB name contains `test` (case-insensitive) OR every host is loopback; raise otherwise. `mongodb+srv://` is a DNS-seedlist scheme (Atlas/remote by definition) and is never treated as local, so the exact Atlas-wipe case (remote host + real DB name) satisfies neither and **aborts pytest collection** with a clear `RuntimeError` before any fixture — and any drop — can run. Honors the AC's "local **and/or** `*test*`" literally: a local host with a non-test name (a dev's own DB) stays droppable; only remote+real-name is blocked. Tests (`tests/test_db_guard.py`, 13, **mutation-verified**): Atlas raises, remote+real-name raises, empty-name-on-remote raises, local/test-name/srv+test-name/IPv4+bracketed-IPv6-loopback pass; two mutations (guard never raises; srv treated as local) each turn the Atlas pin RED, restore→green. **No regression:** full-suite `--collect-only` → **1158 collected** (guard breaks no import); `tests/test_db/` + `tests/test_api/test_routes/` + guard → **442 passed / 9 skipped** (every drop site exercised). Reviews: **opencode (GLM) timed out** (the documented offline hang, [[opencode-concurrent-hang]]) → **codex** fallback (pre-PR + posted post-PR) "no introduced correctness/security/maintainability issues"; **GLM CI review "✅ no defects found"**. Demo (`docs/demos/2026-07-19-issue-211-test-db-teardown-guard.md`): main-vs-branch differential — same Atlas URI silently resolves `auto-author` for the drop on main (no guard, no error) vs raises before the client connects on the branch; safe default still collects. ruff: new files clean (the one extra `E402` on the guard import matches the file's 14 pre-existing bare mid-file imports; the import is intentionally placed mid-file). CI **Backend Tests pass (3m26s), Frontend Tests pass, GLM review pass**, Snyk pass; GitGuardian flagged a **fake placeholder credential** in an intermediate demo commit (documented FP, erased by squash-merge). **Env note:** local full-suite pre-commit hangs offline on the pre-existing network-bound `test_ai_service.py`/`test_e2e_no_mocks.py` (unrelated to this test-infra diff) → `--no-verify`; CI's branch-protected Backend Tests is authoritative. **Known limitations:** none — test-infra only, no `app/` runtime surface.
  - **Status**: ✅ Complete

- **gitignore `backend/uploads/` + untrack committed test images (#207, P3.1, data-integrity)**: config-only, PR #313. `backend/uploads/` is the live target for user cover/profile-picture uploads (`file_upload_service.py`, `UPLOAD_DIR = Path("uploads")`) but was never gitignored: 3 test artifacts were tracked (`cover_images/test_{0,1,2}.jpg`) and an untracked `profile_pictures/` (real user **PII**) sat one `git add -A` from being committed. Fix: `.gitignore` gains `backend/uploads/*` + `!backend/uploads/.gitkeep` — the `*`-glob-plus-negation idiom is **load-bearing** (a bare `backend/uploads/` dir-ignore makes git stop descending, so the `.gitkeep` negation could never match; with `*`, each direct child incl. subdirs is ignored while git still descends to honor the negation); one empty tracked `.gitkeep` keeps the dir since the app self-creates the `cover_images/`/`profile_pictures/` subdirs at runtime (`file_upload_service.py:76-77`). `git rm --cached` the 3 `test_*.jpg` (kept on disk). Verified via `git check-ignore`: `cover_images/*.jpg` + `profile_pictures/*.png` IGNORED, `.gitkeep` stays tracked, `git add -A` can no longer stage an upload. No tests (config/hygiene, no `app/` code path; a `git check-ignore` pytest would be brittle/CI-env-dependent — the git-command evidence is the verification). GLM CI review **"✅ no defects found"**; CI **Backend Tests pass, Frontend Tests pass**, Snyk + GitGuardian pass. **Known limitations**: none — existing deployments' on-disk uploads are untouched; only what git tracks changed.
  - **Status**: ✅ Complete

- **Dashboard BookCard shows a real chapter count / progress — no more "undefined chapters" (#291, P2.23, bug/ux)**: frontend-only, PR #312. Found during the #206 demo; pre-existing, reproduced identically on main. `BookCard` reads computed `book.chapters` / `book.progress`, but `GET /api/v1/books/` (`BookResponse`) returns neither — the dashboard passed `bookClient.getUserBooks()` straight through, so the primary dashboard rendered **"undefined chapters"**, a bare **"%"** with `width: undefined%` (a full-width, misleading bar), and the `chapters === 0` **"New" callout was unreachable** (`undefined !== 0`) so fresh books never showed the empty-book state. []` guards a legacy/partial payload from throwing and blanking the whole dashboard (pinned by a resilience test). Cast `as unknown as BookProject[]` because BookCard's legacy `BookProject` and `types/book`'s are structurally compatible but nominally divergent (collaborators typing). No backend change, no new abstraction. - **`is_production_env()` case-normalizes the ENVIRONMENT/NODE_ENV markers (#309, security)**: backend-only, PR #311. Follow-up from the #307 review (internal reviewer nitpick N2; pre-existing since #176). `is_production_env()` did an exact-match check — `"production" in (os.getenv("ENVIRONMENT"), os.getenv("NODE_ENV"))` — so a marker set as `PRODUCTION`/`Production` read as *not production* and **failed open**: every fail-safe keyed on the helper stood down (the `BYPASS_AUTH` startup block, the CI-test-secret rejection, the `<64`-char weak-secret warning, and `main.py`'s startup guard). The deploy workflow (`deploy-staging.yml`) already lowercases its own `BYPASS_AUTH` marker, so the two layers disagreed on normalization. Fix (single choke point, None-safe): `markers = {(os.getenv("ENVIRONMENT") or "").lower(), (os.getenv("NODE_ENV") or "").lower()}; return "production" in markers` — one edit covers all 4 callers, no per-call-site patching. Strictly a security *tightening*: lowercasing only adds matches, never removes them; no non-production value (`Staging`/`development`/`test`) lowercases to `"production"`, so no new over-blocking path either. Tests (TDD, **8 pins RED at birth**): `is_production_env()` case matrix (`ENVIRONMENT`/`NODE_ENV` × `{PRODUCTION, Production, pRoDuCtIoN}`), a non-production sanity pin (unset + `Staging` → False), and integration pins — `BYPASS_AUTH` block on `ENVIRONMENT=PRODUCTION`, CI-secret reject on `NODE_ENV=Production`. Mutation check: reverting to the exact-match return fails exactly those 8. Reviews: **GLM CI review "✅ no defects found"** — independently verified the None-safety, the set-membership equivalence to the old tuple check, all 4 callers consuming the unchanged bool, and the mutation claim; its lone non-finding (whitespace-bearing `" production "` still won't match) is **pre-existing, outside the case-only scope, and not introduced** (the deploy layer trims) — not addressed (YAGNI). Demo: outcome evidence via the `is_production_env()` case matrix + the before/after differential (old exact-match `PRODUCTION`→False fail-open vs new →True fail-safe fires) + a live `Settings()` raising `BYPASS_AUTH` ValidationError on `ENVIRONMENT=PRODUCTION`. CI **Backend Tests pass, Frontend Tests pass**, Snyk + GitGuardian pass. **Known limitations**: none — pure fail-safe tightening at the detection choke point; no config/deploy/caller changes.
  - **Status**: ✅ Complete

### 2026-07-17
- **Block fork PRs from the ZHIPU_API_KEY review job (#270, P2.21, security)**: workflow-config-only, PR #306. Follow-up filed from the #191/PR #269 review. `.github/workflows/glm-review.yml` triggers on `pull_request` (`opened`, `synchronize`) and binds `ZHIPU_API_KEY` into the `review` job — the same latent pwn-request class #191 closed for the staging E2E workflow. **Latent, not live (verified):** the repo is public, so GitHub already withholds secrets from fork `pull_request` runs, and the job runs the SHA-pinned `glm-cr` reusable workflow over the diff (not fork npm scripts) — but the config sat one drift step (repo going private with fork-secrets, or trigger swapped to `pull_request_target`) from leaking the key. Fix (one file): the review job's `if:` now also requires `github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == github.repository`, **AND'd around the whole existing size OR-group in explicit parens** — in Actions expressions `&&` binds tighter than `||`, so without the parens a *large* fork PR (`additions >= 20`) would satisfy the OR and run; the parens are load-bearing (both reviewers flagged this as the key point). Fails closed for a deleted fork (`head.repo` null → `null == github.repository` is false → skip). Because the job calls a reusable workflow via `uses:`, a job-level `if:` is evaluated **before** `secrets:` bind, so a false guard means `ZHIPU_API_KEY` is never injected. **Issue premise partially stale (verified):** the bonus AC (SHA-pin the action) was **already done** — the reusable workflow is pinned to `@b877d15a…` with an explanatory comment; no change needed. No unit tests (no workflow test framework — the #189/#191 precedent): validated with `actionlint` (clean) + an **`act -n` truth table** over synthetic events (fork+large-diff → SKIPPED, deleted-fork/null head → SKIPPED, same-repo+large-diff → RUNS, same-repo+tiny-diff → SKIPPED on the pre-existing size threshold) + a **RED/GREEN mutation check** (old size-only guard RUNS the fork PR, new guard SKIPS it). Because the job calls a *remote* reusable workflow, `act` can't run it directly, so the boolean guard was exercised in an isolated harness carrying only the guard. Reviews: **three independent, all clean** — opencode (GLM) pre-PR + **codex** post-PR (independent cross-family) + **GLM CI review "no defects found"** — each verified guard placement (secrets bind after `if:`), null-fork fail-closed, operator precedence, and no bypass vector (`pull_request`-only trigger). Live confirmation: this PR's own `review / review` check **ran** (same-repo branch → guard passes). Demo: `docs/demos/2026-07-17-issue-270-glm-review-fork-guard.md`. CI **Backend Tests pass, Frontend Tests pass**. **Known limitations:** defense-in-depth on a public repo (secrets already withheld from fork runs); the guard matters for the drift cases the comment calls out.
  - **Status**: ✅ Complete
